315 — Vector Database ANN Graph Optimizer

Goal: Build, from scratch in Rust, a graph-based approximate-nearest-neighbor (ANN) index optimizer: HNSW + Vamana/DiskANN construction, a graph-quality optimizer (occlusion pruning, degree rebalancing, connectivity repair), a best-first search engine, a distance-computation cost model, and a recall/latency autotuner that hits a target recall@k at minimum cost. Measured against exact KNN and differential-tested vs hnswlib and DiskANN. It is a graph index + optimizer, not a full vector database (that is #45).

Project Number: 315

Project Name: Vector Database ANN Graph Optimizer

Owner: Mujir Qureshi

Document: FINAL_BLUEPRINT.md

Status: specification — locked

1. Goal

Goal: A from-scratch Rust library + annopt CLI that constructs, optimizes, maintains, and searches graph-based ANN indexes (HNSW + Vamana/DiskANN), driving each index to a target recall@k at minimum distance-computations/query via a measurable graph-quality optimizer.

1.1 Engineering definition (what is built)

Single-node, multi-reader Rust workspace whose center of gravity is the graph optimizer: the algorithms deciding directed-edge topology, pruning for navigability, balancing out/in-degree, repairing connectivity after deletes, and autotuning search parameters to a stated operating point. Input: a VectorStore of high-dimensional f32 vectors + a Metric. Output: an optimized navigable graph (Graph → CsrGraph) + a tuned SearchParams, answering k-NN with a measured recall@k.

# Capability Spine locus
1 Scalar + SIMD distances — L2 / InnerProduct / Cosine (f32 AVX2/AVX-512/NEON), DistanceCounter budget ann-core
2 Construction — hierarchical HNSW, flat Vamana/DiskANN, NSG-style repair ann-build
3 Search — greedy_search / beam_search, ef control, RecallHarness recall@k ann-search
4 Topology optimization — occlusion_prune (RobustPrune/RNG/heuristic), degree_rebalance, reverse_edge_repair, connect_components, nsg_spanning_repair, reselect_entry_point ann-optimize
5 Parameter optimization — Autotuner (bisection over ef + grid over α/R/L), CostModel, recall/latency Pareto → TuneResult meeting a RecallTarget ann-optimize
6 Persistence + compression — on-disk CsrGraph, mmap_graph, ProductQuantizer (asymmetric distance), DeleteSet, DiskANN beam-search-with-rerank ann-store

Differentiator vs a bare index: optimization passes are first-class with measured PassReport-style deltas, and tuning is validated against exact KNN ground truth (RecallHarness).

1.2 System boundaries

Trust boundary  : untrusted vectors/queries (dim mismatch, NaN/Inf) validated at the ann-store / CLI API; hard caps (max dim, max degree R, max ef).
Concurrency     : many concurrent read-only searches over an immutable optimized CsrGraph || single writer for incremental insert/delete (Phase 10).
Persistence     : ann-store mmap on-disk image; in-memory Graph is runtime source of truth, file is durable image.

In scope: single node, multi-reader; dense f32 vectors; full build → optimize → search → maintain → tune loop. Out: GPU, distributed/sharded, IVF/clustering-first (PQ only as optional on-disk vector compression).

1.3 Non-goals (explicit) — owners of adjacent scope

NOT this Owner
Full vector DB (SQL, collections, REST, metadata filtering DSL) #45 Vector Database
Generic from-scratch ANN index zoo (HNSW + IVF-PQ as end goal) #255 ANN Index — 315 is the graph quality + optimization layer on top of graph indexes
Embedding model training / inference (vectors arrive pre-computed) #46 Embedding engine

Also excluded: IVF/clustering-first indexes (graph-based only), distributed sharding/replication, GPU kernels. ScaNN-style anisotropic quantization referenced as context only.

1.4 Deployment model & target users

Two artifacts:

  1. Embeddable Rust library — crates ann-core … ann-store linked in-process into a host (retrieval service, RAG backend, embeddings producer). Hot path in-process: zero IPC, zero GC.
  2. CLI annopt — subcommands build · optimize · search · diagnose · tune · bench for offline construction, optimization, tuning sweeps, diagnostics, benchmark reproduction.

Users:

  • Vector-DB engineers embedding an optimized graph index.
  • Retrieval/RAG infra teams needing a recall SLA at bounded distance-computation cost.
  • Search-platform / perf engineers diffing graph quality vs hnswlib/DiskANN and tracing the recall/latency Pareto frontier on ann-benchmarks analogs.

1.5 Phase-0 deliverable — the seven ann-* crates

Phase 0 (## 12) stands up all members, type shells, scalar distance baseline, the RecallHarness, CI, and a smoke test (build a 1k-vector graph, search it, report recall@10 vs brute-force exact KNN):

  1. ann-core — VectorId, VectorStore, Metric {L2,InnerProduct,Cosine}, Distance, scalar+SIMD DistanceKernel, DistanceCounter, DataType (f32 primary; f16/i8 compression). std.
  2. ann-graph — NeighborList (capped + distance-sorted), AdjacencyList, Layer, mutable Graph + immutable CsrGraph, GraphLayout, EntryPoint, (de)serialization. std.
  3. ann-build — HnswBuilder, VamanaBuilder, select_neighbors_heuristic, robust_prune, level assignment, medoid, BuildParams.
  4. ann-optimize — GraphDiagnostics, PruneStrategy, pruning passes, connectivity repair, CostModel, Autotuner. (project center)
  5. ann-search — greedy_search, beam_search, SearchContext (VisitedSet/candidate+result heaps), SearchParams, RecallHarness, filtered+batch search.
  6. ann-store — on-disk graph format, mmap_graph, ProductQuantizer, Tombstone/DeleteSet, DiskANN beam-search-with-rerank.
  7. ann-cli — binary annopt. std.

1.6 What later phases unlock

Phases Heading Unlocks
1–2 ## 13–14 SIMD kernels + DistanceCounter budget; NeighborList/AdjacencyList + CsrGraph storage
3–5 ## 15–17 HNSW build, select_neighbors_heuristic, search engine + recall@k vs harness
6 ## 18 Vamana/DiskANN robust_prune two-pass (α=1 → α∈[1.2,1.4]), medoid entry
7–9 ## 19–21 Optimizer: GraphDiagnostics, occlusion_prune/degree_rebalance/reverse_edge_repair, connect_components/nsg_spanning_repair/reselect_entry_point
10–11 ## 22–23 Incremental insert/delete maintenance; CostModel + Autotuner Pareto search
12–13 ## 24–25 Persistence + PQ + on-disk rerank; conformance / differential / fuzz / regression gates

1.7 Deferred features — gated behind PhaseDeferred("Deferred Features Register")

Feature Gate
GPU distance/build kernels PhaseDeferred("§13")
Distributed / sharded multi-node indexes PhaseDeferred("§14")
Disk-resident billion-scale beyond single-node mmap PhaseDeferred("§24")
Filtered / metadata-constrained search PhaseDeferred("§17")
Learned/adaptive entry-point routing out of scope (see 1.3)

1.8 Timeline (estimate)

  • Phase 0 (skeleton, compiling smoke test): 6–14 weeks, depending on SIMD-kernel maturity and CI.
  • Full system (14 phases): months — Phases 7–11 (the optimizer + autotuner, the project's subject) dominate, then persistence/PQ (12) and the conformance/benchmark gauntlet (13).

1.9 Recommended language + rationale

Rust, edition 2021, MSRV 1.78. std throughout (mmap/threads/CLI).

Requirement Why Rust
Search hot path SIMD distance kernels (AVX2/AVX-512/NEON) + runtime dispatch, zero-cost abstractions, zero GC pauses
Cache-aware layout CSR AdjacencyList / contiguous NeighborList under explicit ownership
Concurrent multi-reader search memory safety over an immutable optimized CsrGraph
Differential testing deterministic seeded builds vs hnswlib / DiskANN

Rejected: C/C++ (memory-unsafe on the concurrent search path; references read, not copied for their unsafety), Go (GC tail-latency pauses; weaker SIMD/layout control), Python (interpreter overhead voids the distance-computation budget; plotting scripts only).

1.10 Prerequisites (systems knowledge demanded)

  • ANN graph algorithms — HNSW (Malkov & Yashunin, TPAMI 2018), Vamana/DiskANN (Subramanya et al., NeurIPS 2019), NSG (Fu et al., VLDB 2019); RNG/occlusion pruning, RobustPrune/α relaxation.
  • Vector search internals — greedy / beam search, dynamic candidate list (ef), recall@k vs latency Pareto reasoning (ann-benchmarks methodology).
  • SIMD & cache — AVX2/AVX-512/NEON f32 kernels, alignment, cache-line locality, CSR adjacency layout.
  • Recall metrics — exact KNN ground truth, recall@k, expected-hops navigability proxy.
  • Product quantization — PQ codebooks, asymmetric distance, re-ranking (Faiss PQ context).

References cited consistently throughout: hnswlib; Microsoft DiskANN/Vamana; NSG; Faiss IndexHNSW; NGT; ann-benchmarks.

2. Why Build This

2.1 Problem statement and the separable layer

Graph-based ANN is the index behind every modern vector DB and RAG stack; this project builds the graph QUALITY + OPTIMIZATION layer (topology decisions, occlusion pruning, connectivity repair, recall/latency autotuning) that the canonical builders (hnswlib, DiskANN) bury inside their construction code — and makes it a first-class, measurable, tunable subsystem.

2.2 Unique systems knowledge taught

Concept What it forces you to internalize
Sub-linear ANN over graphs Why a navigable graph answers k-NN in O(log N) expected hops vs O(N) brute force; convergence as greedy descent on a small-world topology
Navigable small-world theory Long-range + short-range edges → polylog routing; ml = 1/ln(M) level distribution; HNSW as a probabilistic skip-list over space
Recall / latency / memory trilemma No free lunch: higher ef/R buys recall at the cost of distance-comps (latency) and adjacency bytes (memory); the Pareto frontier is the real deliverable
Occlusion pruning select-neighbors heuristic & RobustPrune: keep e only if closer to q than to any selected neighbor → diverse, navigable neighborhood; naive nearest-M creates redundant clustered edges that trap greedy search
Graph connectivity repair Orphans / unreachable components silently destroy recall; reverse_edge_repair, connect_components, nsg_spanning_repair, reselect_entry_point restore reachability-from-EP as an invariant
Distance-computation budget = true cost unit Wall-clock is noisy; DistanceCounter makes cost deterministic, comparable, and the optimization objective. mean_dist_comps ~ ef * mean_out_degree * (1 + hop_factor)

These are the optimizer's concerns — not taught by building "yet another index".

2.3 Why graph ANN matters (historical + architectural)

  • Lineage — NSW → HNSW (Malkov & Yashunin, TPAMI 2018) → Vamana/DiskANN (Subramanya et al., NeurIPS 2019) → NSG (Fu et al., VLDB 2019). Graph methods displaced tree/LSH/IVF as the recall/latency leader on ann-benchmarks.
  • Architectural reality — every production vector DB (Faiss IndexHNSW, NGT, the engines behind RAG retrieval) ships a graph ANN index as default. Understanding the edge-selection and repair layer explains why those systems hit (or miss) their recall SLO.
  • The gap this fills — references hand the optimizer as opaque build code. Here it is explicit: GraphDiagnostics measures it, the pruning/repair passes act on it, the Autotuner searches it — under the OPTIMIZER GUARANTEE (passes only improve-or-preserve recall@k at fixed ef; degree never exceeds R; every node reachable from EP after connect_components).

2.4 Engineering muscles built

  • SIMD distance kernels (AVX2/AVX-512/NEON) with runtime feature dispatch on the search hot path.
  • Cache-aware memory layout: CSR adjacency (CsrGraph), contiguous neighbor lists, versioned VisitedSet reset O(1)/query.
  • Concurrent multi-reader search over an immutable optimized graph under Rust ownership (safety without GC tail-latency).
  • Differential testing & determinism: seeded builds reproducible against hnswlib/DiskANN.
  • Multi-objective optimization: bisection + grid search over (ef, alpha, R, L) to a RecallTarget at minimum cost.
  • Diagnostics as a science: degree histograms, component counting, reachability, navigability score, sampled recall@k.

2.5 Cross-links to portfolio

Project Relationship
45 Vector Database 315 is the index inside a vector DB; 45 adds collections/SQL/REST — explicit non-goals here
46 Embedding / Similarity Produces the vectors 315 consumes; defines Metric (L2/IP/Cosine) semantics
255 ANN HNSW/IVF-PQ The from-scratch index builder; 315 is the quality/optimization layer ON TOP of graph indexes (no IVF/clustering-first here)
251 RAG infra Downstream consumer — retrieval recall@k directly bounds RAG answer quality
84 Graph DB Shares CSR adjacency, traversal, connected-component / reachability machinery
70 Bloom / Count-Min Sketch/bitset intuition reused in VisitedSet and sampled diagnostics
181 SIMD Distance-kernel vectorization shares the AVX/NEON dispatch discipline

2.6 Calibration value

  • A measurable target: recall@10 ≥ 0.95 on SIFT1M/GIST1M/DEEP-subset analogs at competitive mean_dist_comps and p99 latency, validated differentially vs hnswlib + DiskANN.
  • Forces every claim to be quantified against exact KNN via RecallHarness — no hand-waving about "good enough" graphs.
  • Demonstrates staff-level command of the recall/latency/memory trilemma as an explicit, autotuned operating point rather than a fixed default.

2.7 What Mastery Looks Like

Competencies gained, keyed to the phase that instils them. Each bullet names the skill, the evidence of its presence, and the phase that forces it.

  • Phase 1 (SIMD distance kernels): Can write a hand-vectorized AVX2 L2 kernel matching >5 GFLOP/s with 4 FMA accumulators and explain in exact terms why a single-accumulator implementation gives ¼ throughput (FMA latency = 4 cycles, one accumulator serialises the dep chain). Can diff SIMD vs scalar results to within rel 1e-5 and trace any divergence to a specific FMA reordering.
  • Phase 2 (graph representation): Can specify the exact CSR freeze algorithm — prefix-sum of out-degrees into offsets, flatten ids into edges — and explain why the frozen CsrGraph is Send+Sync while AdjacencyList is not, tracing the ownership rule to Rust's aliasing model. Can catch the off-by-one in offsets[n] without running the code.
  • Phase 4 (select-neighbors heuristic): Can reproduce Algorithm 4 from memory, articulate the occlusion predicate d(e,r) < d(e,q) (not d(r,q)), and predict on a 3-point diagram which edge is dropped and which survives. Can explain why keep_pruned backfill is necessary for connectivity in tight clusters.
  • Phase 6 (Vamana / RobustPrune): Can derive why two-pass alpha (1.0 then 1.2–1.4) is superior to single-pass: the first pass seeds a well-connected structure; the second relaxes occlusion to add long-range edges without blowing degree. Can implement robust_prune from the NeurIPS 2019 pseudocode and verify it terminates via the monotone-V-shrink argument.
  • Phase 7–8 (GraphDiagnostics + optimizer passes): Can read a GraphDiagnostics report and identify whether a recall deficit is caused by orphan nodes, over-pruning (too few edges per region), or concentrated hub degree (single over-connected node whose expansion eats the dist-comp budget). Can map each symptom to its repair pass (reverse_edge_repair, connect_components, degree_rebalance).
  • Phase 9 (connectivity repair): Can state the OPTIMIZER GUARANTEE precisely — passes only improve-or-preserve recall@k at fixed ef, out-degree ≤ R, every node reachable from EP post-connect_components — and explain how the snapshot-then-revert mechanism enforces it without a formal proof. Can trace a regression to a specific edge removal via the pre/post diagnostic delta.
  • Phase 11 (CostModel + Autotuner): Can derive the CostModel formula mean_dist_comps ≈ ef · mean_out_degree · (1 + hop_factor) from first principles (one hop expands mean_out_degree nodes, hop_factor accounts for back-tracking), use it to predict the ef required to hit recall@10 ≥ 0.95 on SIFT1M, and identify when the Pareto knee makes raising ef further uneconomical.
  • Phase 12 (PQ + DiskANN): Can explain asymmetric distance computation — query in full precision, database vectors as PQ codes, approximation error bounded by codebook quantisation noise — and predict the recall recovery mechanism: PQ narrows the candidate set, full-precision re-rank recovers the final k. Can size the PQ footprint (m_subspaces × N bytes + codebooks) for 1M vectors and verify it fits in RAM while adjacency stays on SSD.

3. Architectural Overview

3.1 System diagram

    annopt CLI : build | optimize | search | diagnose | tune | bench
        |                  |                 |              |
        v                  v                 v              v
    ann-build         ann-optimize       ann-search     RecallHarness
    HnswBuilder       GraphDiagnostics   greedy_search  (recall@k vs
    VamanaBuilder ->  occlusion_prune    beam_search     exact KNN)
    select_neighbors  degree_rebalance   SearchContext
    robust_prune      connect_components VisitedSet/heaps
        |             reverse_edge_repair    |
        |  builds         CostModel/Autotuner|  reads
        v                 ^   |              v
    ann-graph : Graph / Layer / AdjacencyList / NeighborList / CsrGraph / EntryPoint
        |  vectors + distances                 |  persist / mmap / PQ
        v                                       v
    ann-core : VectorStore · Metric · SIMD DistanceKernel · DistanceCounter
                                               ann-store : on-disk graph · mmap · ProductQuantizer · DeleteSet
 TRUST BOUNDARY ........ untrusted: vector data (dim/NaN/Inf), query vectors, build params
                         (adversarial m/R/L/alpha/ef). Validated/clamped at ann-cli + ann-core/ann-build edges.
 CONCURRENCY BOUNDARY .. mutable Graph + AdjacencyList = single-writer (build/optimize, take &mut).
                         immutable CsrGraph = Send+Sync, N concurrent readers (search), zero locks.
 PERSISTENCE BOUNDARY .. ann-store owns on-disk CsrGraph + PQ codebooks + DeleteSet; mmap_graph is the
                         only file→memory ingress; format-version + checksum gated.
  • Data flow: vectors → ann-build (mutable Graph) → ann-optimize (passes over Graph) → freeze to CsrGraph → ann-search (read-only) / ann-store (persist). RecallHarness scores every stage against exact KNN.
  • &mut direction: flows downward through build/optimize only; search and persistence take & (shared). The freeze Graph → CsrGraph is the one-way gate between writer-world and reader-world.

3.2 Per-box responsibilities

ann-core — substrate. Owns VectorStore (row-major Vec<f32>, dim fixed at construction), VectorId(u32), Metric{L2,InnerProduct,Cosine}, Distance(f32), the scalar+SIMD DistanceKernel trait, DistanceCounter, DataType (f32 primary; f16/i8 for compression). In: raw vector data + Metric. Out: O(1) slice access &[f32] per VectorId, distance scalars. Invariant: every distance respects "smaller = closer" — IP/Cosine stored as negated/1−sim so all heaps order ascending = nearest first; every queried slice's dim equals store dim. Every later phase computes distances only through this layer, so DistanceCounter is the single cost ground truth (Phase 11 CostModel, Phase 13 gates). std.

ann-graph — central graph store, two forms. Mutable build form: AdjacencyList (Vec<NeighborList> indexed by VectorId), NeighborList (SmallVec ids+dists, sorted asc, capped at R), Layer, Graph{layers,entry_point,max_degree,metric,kind}, kind ∈ GraphKind{Hnsw{m,m_max0,ef_construction,ml}, Vamana{r,alpha,l}}. Immutable optimized form: CsrGraph{offsets,edges,entry_point}. In: edges from builders/optimizer. Out: neighbor iteration; (de)serialization. Invariant: NeighborList stays sorted+capped (len ≤ M_max0/R); layers[0] holds all nodes (HNSW); CsrGraph is frozen, never mutated. Phases 2–10 mutate Graph; Phase 5+ search the CsrGraph. std.

ann-build — construction. Owns HnswBuilder, VamanaBuilder, select_neighbors_heuristic (Algorithm 4: keep e iff d(e,q) < d(e,r) ∀ selected r; keep_pruned backfills to M), robust_prune (pop closest p*, add p→p*, drop v where alpha·d(p*,v) ≤ d(p,v), until |out|==R), level assignment (L ~ ⌊−ln(U)·ml⌋, ml=1/ln M), medoid computation, BuildParams{m,m_max0,ef_construction,alpha,r,l,seed}. In: VectorStore + BuildParams. Out: a mutable Graph. Invariant: out-degree never exceeds M_max/R after pruning; bidirectional links consistent; seed makes builds deterministic (differential testing vs hnswlib/DiskANN). Phases 3–4 (HNSW), Phase 6 (Vamana two-pass alpha=1 then 1.2–1.4); Phase 10 reuses single-node insert. Depends on ann-core, ann-graph.

ann-optimize — project center. Owns GraphDiagnostics, PruneStrategy{RobustPrune{alpha}, HeuristicSelect{keep_pruned}, RelativeNeighborhood}, pruning passes occlusion_prune/degree_rebalance/reverse_edge_repair, repair connect_components/nsg_spanning_repair/reselect_entry_point, CostModel, Autotuner (bisection over ef + grid over alpha/R/L → Pareto frontier), RecallTarget, TuneResult. In: a built Graph + VectorStore + diagnostic query sample. Out: an optimized Graph + tuned SearchParams. Invariant — the OPTIMIZER GUARANTEE: passes only improve-or-preserve recall@k at fixed ef on the diagnostic sample (no silent regression); degree never exceeds R; every node remains reachable from entry_point after connect_components. Phases 7–9 (diagnose/prune/repair), Phase 11 (cost/tune). Depends on ann-core, ann-graph, ann-search — it calls search to measure what it optimizes.

ann-search — query engine. Owns greedy_search(&Graph, q, ep, ef, ctx), beam_search(&CsrGraph, q, params, ctx), SearchContext{VisitedSet, candidate heap, result heap}, SearchParams{k,ef,beam_width}, RecallHarness, filtered+batch search. Algorithm: working list W size ef seeded with EP; expand closest-unexpanded, push unvisited neighbors, retain best ef; stop when closest-unexpanded farther than worst in W; return top-k. In: &Graph/&CsrGraph (shared) + query slice + SearchParams. Out: Vec<SearchResult>. Invariant: VisitedSet is a versioned bitset reset in O(1) per query; readers never mutate the graph (& only → safe N-way parallel search). Phase 5 builds this; ann-optimize and RecallHarness call it. Depends on ann-core, ann-graph.

RecallHarness — ground-truth scorer (in ann-search). Owns ground_truth: Vec<Vec<VectorId>> = exact brute-force KNN. In: VectorStore + held-out/self-query sample S + k. Out: estimated recall@k, mean distance-computations, expected-hops proxy (path length to convergence). Invariant: ground truth is computed by exact scan (no approximation), so it is the unbiased reference; recall = |result ∩ truth| / k. Phase 0 introduces it as the baseline; Phases 5/7/11/13 score against it; it is the arbiter for both the optimizer guarantee and the Phase 13 regression gates.

ann-store — persistence + compression. Owns the on-disk CsrGraph format, mmap_graph loader, ProductQuantizer{m_subspaces,ksub,codebooks} (asymmetric distance), Tombstone/DeleteSet{BitVec}, DiskANN-style beam-search-with-rerank. In: a frozen CsrGraph + VectorStore + path. Out: on-disk artifact; MappedGraph (zero-copy). Invariant: on-disk format is version-tagged + checksummed; mmap_graph is the sole file→memory ingress (trust-boundary chokepoint); deletes are logical (DeleteSet) until consolidation. Phase 12 builds it; Phase 10 consumes DeleteSet for tombstone/lazy-relink. Depends on ann-core, ann-graph.

annopt CLI — operator surface, the annopt binary. Subcommands map 1:1 to crates: build→ann-build, optimize/tune→ann-optimize, search→ann-search, diagnose→GraphDiagnostics, bench→RecallHarness+CostModel. In: CLI args + on-disk vectors/graph. Out: graph artifacts, diagnostic reports, recall/latency tables, the Pareto frontier. Invariant: every adversarial arg (m, R, L, alpha, ef, dim) is validated/clamped at this edge before reaching builders; the CLI orchestrates only — no algorithm logic lives here. Exercised end-to-end in the Phase 0 smoke test and Phase 13 conformance benchmarks. Depends on all crates. std.

4. System Constraints

Hard, measured envelope for the annopt binary / ann-* crates. Single-node, in-process Rust library; all budgets are per-machine, per-core where stated. Reference workload unless noted: SIFT1M analog (N=1,000,000, dim=128, f32), single x86-64 host, AVX2 baseline, post-optimize CsrGraph, HNSW m=16/m_max0=32/ef_construction=200. Targets are Phase 13 acceptance/regression gates, not aspirations. NO network/RPC, NUMA, sharding, replication, or distributed budget (§3 non-goals); no GPU.

4.1 Quantified targets

Class Metric Target Workload / condition Origin / derivation
Recall recall@10 ≥ 0.95 SIFT1M, ef tuned vs exact KNN (RecallHarness) spine
Recall recall@100 ≥ 0.90 same op point spine
Recall recall@10 (PQ+rerank) ≥ 0.90 ProductQuantizer m=16, ksub=256, full-precision re-rank spine
Latency search p50 < 250 µs SIFT1M @ recall@10≥0.95, in-mem AVX2 spine
Latency search p95 < 600 µs same spine
Latency search p99 < 1 ms same; alloc-free hot path, no GC spine
Throughput QPS (1 core) ≥ 2,000 SIFT1M @ recall@10 0.95 derived (1/p50)
Throughput QPS (8 cores) ≥ 7× single immutable CsrGraph, lock-free readers derived (≥0.875 eff.)
Dist-comps/query < 2,500 recall@10≥0.95 CostModel ~ ef·mean_out_degree·(1+hop_factor); DistanceCounter spine
Build throughput ≥ 5,000 vec/s/core M=16, ef_c=200, 128-dim; HnswBuilder spine
Build Vamana ≥ 3,000 vec/s/core two-pass α=1.0→[1.2,1.4], R=64, L=100; VamanaBuilder derived
Memory graph edges R·4 B/node CsrGraph.edges = VectorId(u32); R=32→128 B spine
Memory vectors dim·4 B/node VectorStore row-major contiguous spine
Memory total in-mem HNSW R=32 < 1.3× raw-vector bytes raw=N·dim·4; edges+offsets+layers ≤ 0.3× spine
Memory PQ residency ≤ 0.25× raw N·16 B + codebooks; ~32× vs 512 B/vec raw spine
Open mmap_graph (1M) < 200 ms lazy fault-in, no full parse/copy derived
Optimize pass wall time (1M) < 30 s occlusion_prune/degree_rebalance/connect_components derived
Maintenance consolidate amortized < 2× build cost/edge touched re-prune affected neighborhoods only spine
Kernel f32 L2 dim-128 > 5 GFLOP/s eff. AVX2, 32-byte aligned spine

Sizing: per-node steady RAM ≈ R·4 (CSR edges) + dim·4 (vector) + 4 B CSR offset ≈ 644 B at R=32/dim=128. Mutable build form (AdjacencyList/NeighborList) is larger (SmallVec + dists); frozen to CsrGraph for the resident search image. DiskANN/SSD path: only PQ codes (16 B/vec) + codebooks resident; full vectors + adjacency on disk, beam_search batches sector reads + re-ranks.

4.2 Hard safety / DoS caps (enforced once at the API trust boundary)

Cap Limit Failure / rationale
max dim 4096 bounds kernel temp + alignment → Err(DimTooLarge)
max out-degree (R / m_max0) 512 NeighborList SmallVec spill bound; CSR u32 offsets safe
max ef 4096 bounds candidate/result heaps + VisitedSet; caps per-query work
max HNSW layers 16 ml=1/ln(M), L~floor(-ln(U)·ml); clamp tail
max graph order 2^31 VectorId(u32) domain; 1 bit reserved
NaN / Inf vector rejected breaks Distance ordering / heap invariants → Err(NonFinite)
dim mismatch rejected q.len() ≠ dim → Err(DimMismatch)

All checked once at ingress (build/insert/search); hot path (greedy_search/beam_search) assumes validated inputs, no re-checks.

4.3 CPU / cache / SIMD assumptions

  • ISA: x86-64 AVX2+FMA primary; AArch64 NEON secondary; scalar fallback mandatory + differentially tested (Phase 1). Runtime feature dispatch (DistanceKernel); AVX-512 detected, not assumed.
  • Cache: 64 B line; dim-128 f32 vector = 512 B = 8 lines, 32-byte aligned. CsrGraph adjacency contiguous (edges[offsets[v]..offsets[v+1]]) → prefetch-friendly; R=32 neighbor list = 128 B = 2 lines, co-resident.
  • Search is memory-latency bound, not FLOP bound (pointer-chasing descent + beam); tail = cache + branch misses (no allocator, no GC) → p99 < 1 ms achievable and gated.
  • VisitedSet = versioned bitset, O(1) reset/query, zero per-query alloc.
  • Float: IEEE-754 binary32; SIMD reductions not bit-identical to scalar (reassociation) → differential test asserts |Δ| ≤ rel 1e-5, ordering-stable.

4.4 Concurrency envelope

  • Readers: unbounded concurrent searches over immutable CsrGraph; each owns its SearchContext → lock-free, near-linear scaling (≥7× on 8 cores).
  • Writer: single logical writer; insert into optimized graph uses per-node neighbor locks; delete = lazy Tombstone/DeleteSet (no structural lock); consolidation runs under live readers (epoch-deferred frees).
  • Build: parallel HnswBuilder/VamanaBuilder across cores; invariant = same connectivity guarantees as serial build, data-race-free (Rust Send/Sync).

4.5 Recovery / availability framing

Property Guarantee
Source of truth in-mem Graph/CsrGraph; durable image = on-disk format via save_graph/mmap_graph
Corrupt on-disk edge never UB: loader validates header (magic/version/dim/order/checksum), offsets monotonic, edges ∈ [0,len) → typed Err, not panic
Torn write checksum-detected → recover from last good snapshot; tombstones replayed lazily; no WAL in v1 (snapshot-only durability, documented Phase 11 boundary)
Search robustness greedy_search/beam_search never panic on any graph (orphans, cycles, empty layers) — return best-effort top-k
Optimizer guarantee passes only improve/preserve recall@k at fixed ef on diagnostic sample (no silent regression); out-degree never exceeds R; every node reachable from entry_point after connect_components
Fuzz gate (Phase 13) build/search/optimize never panic on arbitrary input
Availability in-process library: no failover/replication/leader election; restart = mmap_graph < 200 ms, not a rebuild

4.6 Classical constraints that DO NOT apply

Constraint Status Reason
Network bandwidth / RPC latency N/A in-process; only I/O is local mmap/SSD (DiskANN path)
FD budget / connection pool / backpressure N/A one mmap fd per index; no sockets, callers invoke functions directly
Rate limiting / quotas N/A no service surface; DoS bounded by §4.2 caps
TLS / auth / multi-tenant isolation N/A trust boundary = input validity, not identity (host's concern)
GC pause / cold-start JIT N/A Rust AOT, no GC; first query may fault-in mmap pages (covered by open budget)
NUMA placement / cross-node consistency N/A single-node, single-process (§3 non-goal: not distributed)

Out-of-scope by spine non-goals (restated as constraints): not a full vector DB (no SQL/collections/REST — project 45), not a generic index zoo (project 255), not an embedding trainer (project 46), not IVF/clustering-first, no GPU.

Cross-cutting rule: no C++ unsafe idiom enters the Rust hot path; determinism (fixed BuildParams.seed) enables byte-identical differential tests vs hnswlib/DiskANN.

5.4 Specifications / Standards

  • No formal RFC/ISO standard exists for ANN vector formats or recall evaluation — state explicitly. De-facto conventions pinned below.
  • fvecs/bvecs/ivecs (SIFT/Texmex, Jégou): little-endian; per vector int32 dim then dim values (f32/u8/i32); GT in .ivecs = top-k neighbor ids/query. ann-store/CLI loaders implement exactly this. Fixtures: SIFT1M / GIST1M / DEEP-subset analogs (Phase 13).
  • HDF5 ann-benchmarks layout (pinned): datasets train/test/neighbors/distances; distance attr ∈ {euclidean, angular, dot} ↔ Metric::{L2, Cosine, InnerProduct}.
  • recall@k protocol (binding for RecallHarness):
    recall@k = (1/|Q|) · Σ_q |Approx_k(q) ∩ Exact_k(q)| / k
Exact_k(q): brute-force KNN baseline (Phase 0), tie-break by VectorId asc
Report tuple: (recall@k, mean_dist_comps, p50_us, p99_us, QPS) at fixed ef
Operating point: e.g. RecallTarget{ k:10, recall:0.95 }; Autotuner returns min-ef Pareto point
  • IEEE 754 binary32: f32 storage + distance accumulation; fixed SIMD accumulation order for tie stability; reject NaN/Inf at the ann-core trust boundary.
  • SIMD ISA refs (pinned): Intel 64 & IA-32 SDM (AVX2/FMA: VFMADD; AVX-512) + Arm NEON (FMLA, FRSQRTE). Phase 1 target: >5 GFLOP/s f32 L2 at dim 128; target_feature gating + scalar fallback contract.
  • POSIX mmap/madvise (MADV_RANDOM for traversal, MADV_WILLNEED for prefetch): mmap_graph + DiskANN sector reads (Phase 12); on-disk CsrGraph regions aligned to 4 KiB / NVMe sector for batched beam reads.
  • Index file header: magic + format version (SemVer); save_graph/mmap_graph reject incompatible versions; per-section CRC32C checksum for crash-recovery integrity on open.

6. Design Principles

Thirteen non-negotiable principles. Each is named so phases (## 12..## 25) cite it (e.g. "per P3-Connectivity"). Order = priority: when two conflict, lower number wins. Each: rule → tradeoff → operational implication → violation failure mode.

P1-RecallMeasuredNeverAssumed

Rule: every build/optimize/tune step touching topology is gated by RecallHarness (exact KNN ground truth) on a fixed sample S (default 1000); GraphDiagnostics.estimated_recall_at_10 is reported, never inferred from degree or navigability_score. Tradeoff: ground-truth KNN is O(N·dim)/query — expensive; amortized by computing once per dataset, caching, sampling S not all queries. Operational: diagnose/tune refuse "improved" without a before/after estimated_recall_at_10 delta; CI fails if recall@10 on SIFT1M analog < 0.95 at tuned ef. Violation: a pass that raises navigability_score but silently drops recall@10 0.95→0.88 ships undetected; KNN answers wrong with no signal.

P2-DistanceComputationIsTheUnitOfCost

Rule: DistanceCounter (AtomicU64) increments on every DistanceKernel::distance call; cost reported in distance-comps/query, never wall-clock alone. CostModel: mean_dist_comps ≈ ef * mean_out_degree * (1 + hop_factor). Tradeoff: atomic increment ~1ns/call; gated behind a flag for the absolute-fastest production path, on by default in bench/tune. Operational: Autotuner optimizes recall vs dist-comps Pareto — machine-independent, reproducible across AVX-512/NEON hosts. Violation: tuning on wall-clock makes the frontier unportable; a "fast" AVX-512 config regresses on NEON, invalidating TuneResult.

P3-OptimizationNeverSilentlyRegressesRecall

Rule: THE OPTIMIZER GUARANTEE — occlusion_prune/degree_rebalance/reverse_edge_repair/connect_components may only improve or preserve recall@k at fixed ef on sample S. Regressing pass is rolled back (graph snapshotted before mutation). Tradeoff: snapshot + re-measure per pass costs a CSR copy + harness run; bounded by a fixed pass order, one check each. Operational: pass returns Result; regression → revert + log, never persist (Phase 8/9). Violation: aggressive alpha removes the only bridge edge to a cluster; recall drops, optimizer reports "success."

P4-OptimizedGraphImmutableSendSync

Rule: CsrGraph is read-only post-optimize; concurrent multi-reader beam_search holds &CsrGraph, mutating only thread-local SearchContext. CsrGraph: Send + Sync. Tradeoff: immutability forces tombstones for deletes (P11) and consolidation rather than in-place edits. Operational: N search threads share one mmap'd graph with zero locks; VisitedSet/candidate-heap/result-heap are per-thread (Phase 12). Violation: mutable shared AdjacencyList under concurrent search → torn NeighborList read, data race, UB; Rust's Sync bound rejects this at compile time — the core reason Rust is chosen over C++.

P5-DegreeIsBounded

Rule: out-degree ≤ R (m_max0 on HNSW layer 0) for every node, always; enforced at insert, prune, repair. Checked via GraphDiagnostics.out_degree_hist; NeighborList capped at R via SmallVec<[_; R]>. Tradeoff: capping can drop a useful edge; mitigated by the heuristic keeping the best R (P7), not the first R. Operational: memory = O(N·R); search dist-comps bounded ⇒ predictable latency + DoS resistance (Phase 8 degree_rebalance). Violation: reverse_edge_repair adding reverse edges without re-pruning makes one hub node with 50k edges; expanding it blows the dist-comp budget and tail latency.

P6-EveryNodeReachableFromEntryPoint

Rule: connectivity invariant — after connect_components, GraphDiagnostics.unreachable_from_ep == 0 and connected_components == 1. reselect_entry_point re-anchors to medoid/centrality when the EP is removed. Tradeoff: repair edges may be lower-quality than heuristic edges; add the minimum NSG-style spanning set, then re-prune (P8). Operational: diagnose reports orphan_count + reachability; gate before declaring a graph optimized (Phase 9). Violation: a deleted hub orphans a cluster; queries near it return only far neighbors, regional recall@k → ~0 while global recall looks fine.

P7-NavigabilityOverRawNearestM

Rule: edge selection uses select_neighbors_heuristic (Alg. 4) / robust_prune diversity — keep e only if d(e,q) < d(e,r) for every selected r — never naive nearest-M. PruneStrategy::{RobustPrune, HeuristicSelect, RelativeNeighborhood} all enforce occlusion. Tradeoff: diverse neighborhoods raise per-query hops slightly but cut total dist-comps by avoiding redundant clustered edges; keep_pruned backfills to R only after diversity is satisfied. Operational: Phase 4 (HNSW heuristic), Phase 6 (Vamana two-pass alpha=1 then 1.2–1.4), Phase 8 (occlusion_prune). Violation: nearest-M on clustered data links each node into one tight cluster; search traps in a local basin, monotonic path breaks, recall stalls ~0.7 regardless of ef.

P8-DeterminismViaSeededRng

Rule: all randomness — level assignment L ~ floor(-ln(U)·ml), ml = 1/ln(M), sampling, tie-breaks — flows from BuildParams.seed; same seed + same vectors ⇒ bit-identical Graph/CsrGraph bytes. Tie-breaks order by (Distance, VectorId), never HashMap iteration. Tradeoff: forgoes entropy-based load spreading and constrains parallel build to deterministic merges (some throughput cost); acceptable single-node. Operational: enables differential tests vs hnswlib/DiskANN and golden-bytes regression gates (Phase 13); bug repro = re-run the recorded seed. Violation: thread-nondeterministic insert order → different topology each run → flaky recall, irreproducible regression, golden-bytes flake.

P9-SimdOnHotPathScalarAsOracle

Rule: runtime-dispatched AVX2/AVX-512/NEON kernels serve search; the scalar DistanceKernel is the correctness oracle. SIMD must match scalar within relative 1e-5 across all Metric variants. Tradeoff: maintaining N kernels; justified — distance dominates the search budget (P2). Operational: Phase 1 micro-bench + differential test (SIMD vs scalar) per distance variant; metric dispatch resolved once per query, not per comparison. Violation: FMA-reordered AVX-512 L2 disagrees with scalar past tolerance → neighbor ordering flips → graph built on host A scores differently on host B.

P10-SearchNeverPanicsOnAdversarialInput

Rule: greedy_search/beam_search are total over malformed input — empty graph, dim mismatch, NaN/Inf query, ef < k, dangling entry_point. Return empty/Err, never panic, never UB. Validation at the call boundary, not per hop. Tradeoff: a few branches per query entry; one-time cost. Operational: Phase 13 fuzzer asserts no panic across build/search/optimize; NaN/Inf vectors rejected at insert. Violation: NaN distance poisons heap ordering (partial_cmp → None) → BinaryHeap corrupts, greedy search loops or unwrap-panics, taking down the search process and every concurrent reader.

P11-TombstonesNotInPlaceDeletes

Rule: deletes set DeleteSet bits + Tombstone; edges remain until consolidation. Search filters tombstoned ids from results yet still traverses through them. Deletes are O(1), never synchronously rewrite the graph. Tradeoff: stale edges waste a little traversal until compaction (recall/latency drift); required because CsrGraph/PQ vectors are immutable/mmap'd (P4, P10-store). Operational: lazy relink on next optimize; consolidation rewrites CSR dropping tombstones + re-pruning (re-applying P6 connectivity, P7 prune) — Phase 10/12. Violation: in-place edge removal on a live mmap'd graph tears neighbors' adjacency mid-traversal → readers see garbage NeighborList slices; UB and broken reachability.

P12-TuneToATargetReportThePareto

Rule: Autotuner finds the smallest ef meeting a RecallTarget (bisection) then sweeps (alpha, R, L) grid; output is the full recall/latency Pareto frontier (Vec<TuneResult>), never a single magic number. Tradeoff: grid search is O(grid·S·query-cost); bounded by a coarse grid + harness sampling. Operational: caller picks the operating point (e.g. recall@10 ≥ 0.95 at min dist-comps); tune never hides the curve (Phase 11). p99_us reported per TuneResult. Violation: reporting one "best" config hides that recall@10=0.99 costs 4× the dist-comps of 0.95 — caller over-pays or under-delivers the SLA.

P13-GenericOverMetric

Rule: every algorithm (build, prune, search, diagnostics) operates through Metric + Distance; InnerProduct/Cosine stored as negated/1-sim so "smaller = closer" holds in all heaps uniformly. Tradeoff: the distance-convention indirection; eliminated by normalizing at the kernel boundary, not per comparison (P9). Operational: one heap rule (ascending = nearest) across L2/IP/Cosine; no metric-specific branch in beam_search's inner loop. Violation: an IP path maximizing raw similarity inverts heap order vs L2 → candidate/result heaps pop the wrong end → garbage KNN under Cosine while L2 tests pass.

7. System Invariants

Invariants that MUST hold for every Graph, CsrGraph, optimizer pass, and search. Format per invariant: statement · why · enforced-where · detection · telemetry · recovery · debug. I0 = structural (build/optimize time), I1 = runtime (search), I2 = persistence. debug_assert! everywhere; cfg(feature="invariant-checks") upgrades to assert! in CI + fuzzing. GraphDiagnostics is the standing observability surface; annopt diagnose re-checks all of them.

7.1 Invariant catalog

# Invariant (one line) Class
INV-1 NeighborList sorted ascending by Distance; len() <= R (M_max0 on layer 0) I0
INV-2 Every directed edge targets a live (non-tombstoned) VectorId I0/I1
INV-3 Graph connected: all live nodes reachable from entry_point I0
INV-4 HNSW layer containment: node on layer L present on all layers < L; EP on top layer I0
INV-5 Distance convention: smaller = closer for L2/InnerProduct/Cosine; all heaps order asc I0/I1
INV-6 Optimizer guarantee: passes never reduce sampled recall@k at fixed ef I0
INV-7 Search is pure read over immutable CsrGraph; no alloc in steady-state hot loop I1
INV-8 VisitedSet resets correctly per query — zero cross-query contamination I1
INV-9 save→mmap→search reproduces in-memory results bit-for-bit I2
INV-10 robust_prune / select_neighbors_heuristic always terminate, respect R I0
INV-11 DistanceCounter monotone non-decreasing within a query; reset between queries I1
INV-12 CsrGraph.offsets monotone non-decreasing, offsets[n]==edges.len() I0/I2

7.2 INV-1 — NeighborList sorted + capped

  • Statement: nl.dists non-decreasing; nl.ids.len() == nl.dists.len() <= R_max (M per-layer, M_max0 layer 0, R Vamana).
  • Why: search-layer expansion + heuristic pruning assume sorted nearest-first; cap bounds per-query distance comps (CostModel mean_out_degree) and SmallVec inline capacity.
  • Enforced: NeighborList::insert keeps order; select_neighbors_heuristic / robust_prune / degree_rebalance truncate to R; debug_assert_sorted() post each mutation.
  • Detection: GraphDiagnostics.out_degree_hist[64] — bucket > R ⇒ violation; verify_sorted() scan in diagnose.
  • Telemetry: out_degree_hist, mean_out_degree; trace span prune.truncate{node,before,after}.
  • Recovery: re-sort + truncate (idempotent); degree_rebalance(g, R) repairs over-degree.
  • Debug: dump offending VectorId adjacency; diff against builder's pre-prune candidate pool.

7.3 INV-2 — Edges point to live nodes

  • Statement: ∀ edge u→v: v < store.len ∧ !delete_set.contains(v).
  • Why: dereferencing a tombstoned/OOB id ⇒ stale distance or panic; deletes use lazy relink so dangling edges accumulate until consolidation.
  • Enforced: delete only tombstones (no edge rewrite); beam_search/greedy_search skip tombstoned ids; consolidation physically drops them, then reverse_edge_repair.
  • Detection: diagnose counts edges into DeleteSet; OOB caught by bounds check (panic in CI, skip in release fuzz).
  • Telemetry: dangling_edge_count, orphan_count; trace search.skip_tombstone{id}.
  • Recovery: consolidation rebuilds CsrGraph excluding tombstones; relink predecessors via connect_components.
  • Debug: fixture — delete 10% of SIFT subset, assert recall holds, consolidate, re-check edge targets.

7.4 INV-3 — Connectivity from entry_point

  • Statement: after connect_components, BFS from entry_point reaches every live node; connected_components == 1, unreachable_from_ep == 0.
  • Why: unreachable node ⇒ permanent recall@k loss (never returnable); the core optimizer correctness property.
  • Enforced: connect_components(g, store) links each non-EP component's medoid into the EP component; nsg_spanning_repair ensures spanning; reselect_entry_point keeps EP central.
  • Detection: GraphDiagnostics.{connected_components, unreachable_from_ep, orphan_count} via union-find + reachability scan.
  • Telemetry: connected_components, unreachable_from_ep; trace repair.connect{from_comp,to_node}.
  • Recovery: rerun connect_components; if EP itself orphaned (post-delete), reselect_entry_point first.
  • Debug: diagnose --dump-components; smallest-component nodes are the repair targets.

7.5 INV-4 — HNSW layer containment

  • Statement: node assigned level L ⇒ present on layers 0..=L; entry_point on top layer; layers[0] holds all live nodes.
  • Why: descending-layer entry requires upper-layer nodes to exist on all lower layers; broken containment ⇒ search drops into a hole.
  • Enforced: HnswBuilder inserts node into every layer 0..=L at assignment time; EP updated only when a strictly higher level appears. (Vamana single-layer: trivially holds.)
  • Detection: per-layer subset check layer[i].nodes ⊆ layer[i-1].nodes; EP-on-top assert.
  • Telemetry: per-layer node counts, top_level; trace insert.level_assign{id,L,ml}.
  • Recovery: rebuild layer membership from base + level vector (deterministic from seed); no in-place fix.
  • Debug: verify ml == 1/ln(M); assigned-level histogram should match geometric decay.

7.6 INV-5 — Distance ordering convention

  • Statement: Distance(smaller) == closer for all Metric; IP/Cosine stored negated/1-sim; every BinaryHeap orders by Distance ascending (candidate heap via Reverse).
  • Why: mixing conventions silently inverts search; one rule everywhere kills the whole bug class.
  • Enforced: DistanceKernel::distance returns converted Distance; constructors normalize Cosine inputs; heap types fixed in SearchContext.
  • Detection: property test metric_monotone — closer vector ⇒ smaller Distance across scalar + SIMD; SIMD-vs-scalar agreement within 1e-5.
  • Telemetry: metric tag on every span; n/a at steady state.
  • Recovery: none at runtime — compile/test-time discipline.
  • Debug: differential vs brute-force exact KNN (RecallHarness) — inversion shows as ~0 recall.

7.7 INV-6 — Optimizer guarantee (no recall regression)

  • Statement: for any pass (occlusion_prune/degree_rebalance/reverse_edge_repair/connect_components): recall@k(after, ef) >= recall@k(before, ef) on the fixed diagnostic sample (within tolerance epsilon); out_degree <= R preserved.
  • Why: project's headline contract — optimization may only improve or preserve quality, never silently regress.
  • Enforced: each pass measures estimated_recall_at_10 on sample S before/after; regression beyond epsilon ⇒ revert (operate on clone or keep undo log).
  • Detection: RecallHarness diff at fixed ef; CI regression gate.
  • Telemetry: estimated_recall_at_10, navigability_score, mean_dist_comps per pass; trace optimize.pass{name,recall_before,recall_after}.
  • Recovery: automatic revert-to-snapshot on regression; surface failing pass in report.
  • Debug: bisect sample queries that regressed; inspect which pruned edges they relied on.

7.8 INV-7 — Pure-read immutable search

  • Statement: beam_search(&CsrGraph, ...) takes & (shared); no mutation of graph/store; steady-state hot loop allocates nothing beyond reuse of caller-supplied &mut SearchContext (heaps/VisitedSet pre-sized to ef).
  • Why: enables lock-free concurrent multi-reader search; hot-path allocations destroy tail latency (p99 target).
  • Enforced: CsrGraph immutable by type; SearchContext reused across queries; SmallVec/pre-reserved heaps.
  • Detection: dhat/alloc-counting test asserts 0 heap allocs across N queries after warmup; & signature is compile-time proof of no mutation.
  • Telemetry: alloc_count_per_query (test), p50/p99_us; trace search.alloc must never fire.
  • Recovery: n/a (correctness by construction); perf regression ⇒ fail bench gate.
  • Debug: flamegraph + dhat to locate stray alloc (usually a missed clear() vs re-new()).

7.9 INV-8 — VisitedSet per-query reset

  • Statement: versioned bitset — bump generation counter for O(1) logical clear; node "visited" iff stamp == current generation. No state leaks across queries.
  • Why: stale visited bits ⇒ skipped neighbors ⇒ recall loss; a full memset per query would dominate latency.
  • Enforced: SearchContext bumps generation at query start; generation overflow (u32 wrap) triggers a real zero-fill.
  • Detection: test — run query A then B sharing one context; B's result must equal B-on-fresh-context.
  • Telemetry: visited_generation, visited_full_reset_count (overflow events, rare).
  • Recovery: overflow path zero-fills + resets generation — always correct, occasionally slow.
  • Debug: interleave two query streams; any divergence localizes the missing bump.

7.10 INV-9 — Persistence round-trip determinism

  • Statement: save_graph → mmap_graph → beam_search yields identical Vec<SearchResult> (same ids, same Distance bits) as the in-memory CsrGraph for the same query + SearchParams.
  • Why: on-disk path must not change answers; bit-for-bit guards endianness, padding, float drift, PQ-vs-full-precision confusion.
  • Enforced: fixed little-endian layout, explicit alignment in MappedGraph; offsets/edges copied verbatim; PQ path always full-precision re-ranks top candidates.
  • Detection: round-trip differential test (in-mem vs mmap) on every metric + dataset analog; checksum header.
  • Telemetry: format magic/version, crc32; trace store.mmap{path,bytes}.
  • Recovery: version/crc mismatch ⇒ refuse load (no silent reinterpret).
  • Debug: hexdump header; compare offsets[] prefix; PQ recall gap isolates re-rank bugs.

7.11 INV-10 — Prune/heuristic termination + degree bound

  • Statement: robust_prune loop strictly shrinks V each iteration (pop closest, remove occluded) and halts at |out(p)| == R or V empty; select_neighbors_heuristic scans candidates once. Both return len() <= R.
  • Why: an optimizer that loops forever or overflows degree is unusable; bounds build-time cost.
  • Enforced: monotone V decrease (loop variant), explicit R cap; keep_pruned backfill cannot exceed R.
  • Detection: fuzz build/optimize "never panic, never hang" with watchdog; post-condition out_degree <= R.
  • Telemetry: prune_iterations, resulting out_degree; trace robust_prune{p,alpha,kept,removed}.
  • Recovery: n/a — termination is structural; degree overflow caught by debug_assert.
  • Debug: log V size per iteration; non-monotone shrink ⇒ occlusion-removal bug (alpha*d(p*,v) <= d(p,v)).

7.12 INV-11/INV-12 — Counter monotonicity + CSR well-formedness

  • INV-11: DistanceCounter increments only (per query), read into mean_dist_comps, reset at query boundary. Why: CostModel/Autotuner trust it as the cost signal. Detect: counter == actual kernel calls (test hook). Recover: reset on context init.
  • INV-12: CsrGraph.offsets length n+1, non-decreasing, offsets[0]==0, offsets[n]==edges.len(); edges[i] < store.len. Why: malformed CSR ⇒ OOB neighbor slice. Enforced in Graph→CsrGraph lowering + mmap validation. Detect: structural scan in diagnose/load. Recover: refuse to load; rebuild from mutable Graph. Debug: assert each offsets[i+1]-offsets[i] <= R.

7.13 Enforcement matrix

Invariant Build Optimize Search Persist CI gate
INV-1 ✓ ✓ r — assert+hist
INV-2 ✓ ✓ skip ✓ fuzz
INV-3 ✓ ✓ — — reachability
INV-4 ✓ — r — subset-check
INV-5 ✓ ✓ ✓ — proptest
INV-6 — ✓ — — recall-diff
INV-7 — — ✓ — dhat alloc
INV-8 — — ✓ — interleave
INV-9 — — r ✓ roundtrip
INV-10 ✓ ✓ — — fuzz+postcond
INV-11 — r ✓ — counter-eq
INV-12 ✓ ✓ r ✓ struct-scan

✓ = mutated/established here · r = relied upon (read) · skip = tolerated via runtime skip · — = n/a. All assert!-class checks compile out in release except behind feature="invariant-checks" (always on in CI + fuzz targets).

8. Domain Model — Vectors, Metrics, and the Navigable Graph

The heart of the spec. Defines the four nouns the whole system manipulates: vectors, metrics/distances, the navigable graph (HNSW layers + Vamana flat), and the diagnostics/optimizer view over that graph. Every later phase (## 12–## 25) speaks in these terms.

8.1 Vector + Metric Model

VectorStore — ann-core. Row-major, contiguous, single allocation.

pub struct VectorId(pub u32);             // dense, 0..len; index into store + adjacency
pub struct VectorStore { dim: usize, data: Vec<f32>, len: u32 } // data.len() == dim*len
Fact Value / rule
Layout row i = data[i*dim .. (i+1)*dim]; slice fetch O(1), cache-line-aligned base
VectorId range 0 ..= len-1; VectorId(v) >= len ⇒ OutOfBounds
Alignment base ptr 64-byte aligned (AVX-512); dim need not be lane-multiple — kernels tail-handle
Mutability append-only at build; immutable + Sync during search (multi-reader, lock-free)
DataType f32 primary (search hot path); f16 storage-halving; i8 scalar-quant for on-disk PQ path only

Metric + Distance + convention. All ordering is "smaller = closer, nearest-first" everywhere — heaps, NeighborList, pruning. IP/Cosine are converted to a distance so the invariant holds globally.

pub enum Metric { L2, InnerProduct, Cosine }
pub struct Distance(pub f32);   // monotone in true distance; comparison-only for IP/Cosine
Metric Stored Distance Note
L2 ‖a−b‖² (squared, no sqrt) sqrt unneeded — monotone; saves 1 op/comp
InnerProduct −⟨a,b⟩ (negated) larger sim ⇒ smaller Distance; not a true metric (no triangle ineq)
Cosine −⟨â,b̂⟩, vectors L2-normalized at insert reduces to IP on unit vectors

DistanceKernel contract — ann-core:

pub trait DistanceKernel { fn distance(&self, a: &[f32], b: &[f32]) -> Distance; }
  • Pre: a.len() == b.len() == dim; both finite (NaN/Inf rejected at ingest, never reach kernel).
  • Determinism: fixed (a,b,Metric,dim) ⇒ identical result across SIMD/scalar within tolerance; differential tests gate |simd − scalar| ≤ 1e-5·‖·‖.
  • Side effect: each call bumps DistanceCounter (AtomicU64) — the per-query budget unit the CostModel/Autotuner optimize against.

8.2 The Navigable Graph as a Formal Object

G = (V, E), directed. V = {VectorId(0..len)}. E ⊆ V×V. out(v) = adjacency of v; invariant |out(v)| ≤ R (R = max_degree, = M_max0 on HNSW layer 0). Edge (u→v) caches Distance(u,v); NeighborList sorted ascending.

pub struct NeighborList { ids: SmallVec<[VectorId; R]>, dists: SmallVec<[Distance; R]> } // sorted asc, capped R
pub struct AdjacencyList { neighbors: Vec<NeighborList> }   // index = VectorId; mutable build form
pub struct Layer { adjacency: AdjacencyList, level: u8 }

HNSW = stack of Layers. layers[0] = base = all nodes; layers[ℓ] ⊂ layers[ℓ−1] (nested). Level assignment per insert:

L = floor(-ln(U) * ml),   U ~ Uniform(0,1],   ml = 1 / ln(M)

Geometric-tail: P(level ≥ ℓ) = exp(−ℓ/ml); expected layers ≈ ln(len)/ln(M). Entry at entry_point = highest node. Degree caps: M (build target), M_max (ℓ≥1), M_max0 = 2M (ℓ=0).

Vamana/DiskANN = single flat graph (no layers), entry = medoid (argmin Σdist, sampled). Uniform cap R; build list L; occlusion relaxation alpha ∈ [1.2,1.4].

pub enum GraphKind { Hnsw{m,m_max0,ef_construction,ml}, Vamana{r,alpha,l} }
pub struct Graph { layers: Vec<Layer>, entry_point: VectorId, max_degree: u32, metric: Metric, kind: GraphKind }

AdjacencyList ↔ CsrGraph duality. Build = mutable AdjacencyList (per-node SmallVec, push/prune cheap). Optimize finalizes → immutable CSR: cache-dense, zero per-node alloc, mmap-friendly.

pub struct CsrGraph { offsets: Vec<u32>, edges: Vec<VectorId>, entry_point: VectorId }
// out(v) = edges[offsets[v] .. offsets[v+1]];  offsets.len()==len+1;  edges.len()==Σ|out(v)|
AdjacencyList (build)              CsrGraph (search/disk)
 node0: [3,7,1]                    offsets: [0,3,5,8,...]
 node1: [0,4]            ── CSR ─▶ edges:   [3,7,1, 0,4, ...]
 node2: [5,2,6]                    entry_point: medoid/top
 (alloc per node, mutable)         (2 flat Vecs, immutable, Sync)

8.3 Construction Algorithms (pseudo-code)

HNSW insert (HnswBuilder, Phase 3–4):

insert(q):
  L = floor(-ln(rand()) * ml);  ep = entry_point
  for ℓ in top_level .. L+1:          # descend, greedy ef=1
     ep = search_layer(q, {ep}, ef=1, ℓ).nearest
  for ℓ in min(L,top_level) .. 0:     # connect on each owned layer
     W = search_layer(q, {ep}, ef_construction, ℓ)
     N = select_neighbors_heuristic(W, M, extend=true, keep_pruned)
     add_bidirectional(q, N, ℓ)
     for r in N: if |out(r,ℓ)| > M_max(ℓ): out(r,ℓ)=select_neighbors_heuristic(out(r,ℓ), M_max(ℓ), ..)
     ep = W.nearest
  if L > top_level: top_level=L; entry_point=q

search_layer (inner best-first, also the search-engine kernel):

search_layer(q, seeds, ef, ℓ):
  visited=seeds;  C=min-heap(seeds by d(·,q));  W=max-heap(seeds, cap ef)
  while C nonempty:
     c = C.pop_nearest()
     if d(c,q) > W.worst: break               # closest unexpanded ≥ worst kept ⇒ stop
     for e in out(c,ℓ):
        if e in visited: continue
        visited.add(e)
        if d(e,q) < W.worst or |W|<ef: C.push(e); W.push(e); if |W|>ef: W.pop_worst()
  return W                                     # ef nearest

select-neighbors heuristic (Algorithm 4) — diversity, not naive nearest-M:

select_neighbors_heuristic(cands_sorted_by_d_to_q, M, extend, keep_pruned):
  R=[]; discarded=[]
  for e in cands:                       # nearest-first
     if |R|==M: break
     if forall r in R: d(e,q) < d(e,r): R.push(e)   # keep iff closer to q than to any kept r
     else: discarded.push(e)
  if keep_pruned: while |R|<M and discarded: R.push(discarded.pop_nearest())  # backfill
  return R

Vamana robust_prune + two-pass build (VamanaBuilder, Phase 6):

robust_prune(p, V, alpha, R, store):     # V = visited pool from greedy_search(p)
  V.remove(p); out(p)=[]
  while V nonempty:
     p* = argmin_{v in V} d(p,v);  out(p).push(p*)
     if |out(p)| == R: break
     V.retain(|v| alpha * d(p*,v) > d(p,v))    # occlude v if p* dominates (relaxed by alpha≥1)
  return out(p)

build_vamana:
  init random R-regular graph; medoid = sampled_argmin Σ d
  for alpha in [1.0, alpha_final]:           # pass1 tight; pass2 alpha∈[1.2,1.4] long-range
     for p in random_perm(V):
        V = greedy_search(medoid, p, L).visited
        out(p) = robust_prune(p, V, alpha, R)
        for j in out(p): add j→p; if |out(j)|>R: out(j)=robust_prune(j, out(j)∪{p}, alpha, R)

8.4 Search Algorithm (pseudo-code)

pub struct SearchParams { k: u32, ef: u32, beam_width: u32 }   // invariant ef >= k
pub struct SearchContext { visited: VisitedSet, candidates: BinaryHeap<Reverse<(Distance,VectorId)>>, results: BinaryHeap<(Distance,VectorId)> }

beam_search(CsrGraph) = search_layer over flat CSR with ef/beam_width; greedy_search(Graph) walks HNSW layers top→0 (ef=1 above, ef=ef_search at layer 0), returns top-k. VisitedSet = versioned bitset: per-query epoch:u32; "visited" iff stamp[id]==epoch; reset = epoch+=1 ⇒ O(1) across queries (no clear). Stop: closest unexpanded farther than worst-of-ef ⇒ monotone search path terminates.

8.5 Optimizer Model (ann-optimize, project center)

pub struct GraphDiagnostics {
  mean_out_degree: f32, out_degree_hist: [u32;64], min_in_degree: u32, orphan_count: u32,
  connected_components: u32, unreachable_from_ep: u32, navigability_score: f32, estimated_recall_at_10: f32 }
Field Meaning / red flag
orphan_count in-degree 0 → unreachable; must be 0 after repair
unreachable_from_ep BFS from EP misses them → recall ceiling < 1
connected_components >1 ⇒ disconnected; connect_components reduces to 1
navigability_score sampled expected-hops proxy (lower = more navigable)
estimated_recall_at_10 sampled queries vs exact KNN (RecallHarness)

Passes (Phase 8–9): occlusion_prune(g,strat,store) (RobustPrune/RNG/heuristic edge thinning); degree_rebalance(g,R) (cap to R via heuristic re-select); reverse_edge_repair(g,min_in_degree) (add reverse edges to lift low/zero in-degree nodes); connect_components(g,store) (link unreachable subgraphs to EP component via nearest cross-edges); reselect_entry_point(g,store)→VectorId (medoid/centrality).

Optimizer guarantee (LOCKED invariant): every pass MUST (1) not regress estimated_recall_at_k at fixed ef on the diagnostic sample, (2) keep |out(v)| ≤ R ∀v, (3) leave every node reachable from entry_point after connect_components. Violation ⇒ pass rejected, graph rolled back.

8.6 Worked Example — 6 nodes in 2-D

Points: A(0,0) B(1,0) C(0,1) D(5,5) E(6,5) F(5,6). Clusters {A,B,C} {D,E,F}. L2² from B(1,0), M=2:

cand sorted by d(·,B):  A(1)  C(2)  D(41)  E(50)  F(52)
Selection out(B) Why
naive nearest-M {A, C} both intra-cluster → B reaches {D,E,F} only via A/C; long path
heuristic (Alg.4) {A, D} A kept (nearest); C occluded (d(C,B)=2, not closer to B than to A); D kept (d(D,B)<d(D,A)) → long-range edge

Heuristic trades a redundant short edge (C, reachable via A) for a long-range edge to the far cluster → navigable across clusters at degree 2.

Search query q(0.5,0.5), EP=A, ef=2, k=1. out: A→{B,C}, B→{A,D}, C→{A,F}:

step  expand  push(unvisited)   W(best-2)         dist_comps
 0    seed A                    [A:0.5]            1
 1    A       B(0.5),C(0.5)     [A:0.5,B:0.5]      3
 2    B       D(40.5)           [A:0.5,B:0.5]      4   D worse than worst
 3    C       F(60.5)           [A:0.5,B:0.5]      5
stop: closest unexpanded D(40.5) > W.worst(0.5)
result top-1 = A (true NN of q; A,B tie) ; recall@1 = 1.0 ; dist_comps ≈ 5

8.7 Malformed / Adversarial Inputs → Error

Input Detected at Error
zero vectors (len==0) build EmptyStore — no medoid, no EP
dim mismatch (query.len ≠ dim) search/insert DimensionMismatch{expected,got}
NaN/Inf in a vector ingest validation NonFiniteValue{id,component} — NaN breaks heap ordering; rejected pre-kernel
R == 0 BuildParams validate InvalidDegree — no edges, graph trivially disconnected
ef < k SearchParams validate EfBelowK{ef,k} — cannot return k from ef-list
disconnected (components>1) diagnose/optimize Disconnected{components} → triggers connect_components; surfaced if repair off
self-loop v→v build/insert assert SelfLoop{id} — wastes a degree slot, d=0 trap; forbidden invariant

8.8 HNSW Descend-then-Search (sequence)

Query q,k,ef            greedy_search        search_layer        Store/Kernel
   |  search(q,k,ef) ------> |                    |                   |
   |                         | ep=entry_point     |                   |
   |        for ℓ=top..1     |-- search_layer(ef=1,ℓ) -->|            |
   |                         |                    |-- d(c,q) -------->| (++DistanceCounter)
   |                         |<-- nearest ep -----|                   |
   |        ℓ=0              |-- search_layer(ef=ef_search,0) -->|    |
   |                         |                    |-- expand/visit/heap
   |                         |<-- W (ef nearest) -|                   |
   |<-- top-k SearchResult --|                    |                   |

Descend cheaply (ef=1) through sparse upper layers to land near q, then spend the ef budget only on the dense base layer — why mean_dist_comps ≈ ef · mean_out_degree · (1 + hop_factor) stays small relative to recall.

9. Tech Stack ADR Table

Architecture Decision Records. Each row is LOCKED for the lifetime of the project. ADR # is stable; supersession requires a new row, never an in-place edit. Cross-reference: crate split → "Workspace Crates"; algorithms → "Key Algorithms"; targets → "Constraints".

ADR # Decision Locked Choice Rejected Alternatives Rationale When the Rejected Choice Wins
01 Implementation language Rust 2021, MSRV 1.78 C/C++; Go; Python Zero-cost SIMD on search hot path; explicit cache-aware layout under ownership; memory-safe concurrent multi-reader search; deterministic builds. C/C++ unsafe on concurrent path (refs hnswlib/DiskANN read, not copied); Go GC pauses wreck tail latency + weak layout control; Python interpreter voids the distance-budget meaning (plotting only). C/C++ wins when you need to call into a legacy BLAS/MKL DSP library with no Rust bindings, or when the entire team is expert C++ and has zero Rust experience — onboarding cost exceeds safety benefit at small team sizes. Python wins for a pure-notebook research prototype run once on a laptop.
02 Workspace shape 7 ann-* crates (core/graph/build/optimize/search/store/cli) Single monolith crate; per-algorithm crates Enforces dep DAG (core←graph←{build,search}←optimize); isolates SIMD/unsafe to ann-core; parallel compile; ann-optimize is the project center, kept its own crate. A single monolith wins when the project has one author, compile-time is the bottleneck, and the crate boundary ceremony exceeds its value — e.g. a rapid prototype that won't be published to crates.io or embedded by others.
03 Graph families HNSW + Vamana/DiskANN (RobustPrune), NSG-style repair IVF/clustering-first; LSH; tree (KD/ball) Project is the graph QUALITY+OPTIMIZATION layer over graph indexes; IVF is project 255's clustering path; LSH/trees not graph-navigable, no edge-pruning surface to optimize. PQ kept only as on-disk vector compression. IVF/clustering-first wins for very high-cardinality, low-recall workloads (top-100 with recall@100 ≥ 0.80 acceptable) where build time must be sub-minute and the corpus is static — no optimizer needed. LSH wins for streaming high-dimensional binary/Hamming data where graph construction itself is too slow.
04 Build vs search graph form Mutable AdjacencyList (build) → immutable CsrGraph (search) Single mutable form for both; lock-free concurrent mutable graph Build needs O(1) edge churn (index=VectorId); search needs contiguous cache-friendly traversal (CSR offsets+edges). Freeze boundary lets search be lock-free over immutable data. A single mutable form wins when the index must accept continuous high-rate inserts with no offline freeze window (e.g. a live streaming index with sub-second freshness SLA) — the freeze latency becomes unacceptable. Lock-free concurrent mutable structures (skip-list adjacency) win in that streaming regime.
05 Per-node neighbor container NeighborList = SmallVec<[_;R]> capped + distance-sorted asc Vec; BinaryHeap; B-tree R small/known → inline SmallVec avoids per-node heap alloc; sorted-asc enables early-exit occlusion checks + cheap M_max truncation; heap wastes locality, B-tree overkill for ≤R items. A BinaryHeap wins during construction when you perform many candidate insertions before a final selection pass and don't need sorted order until the end — the heap avoids O(R) shifts per insert. A Vec with sort-on-demand wins when R is large (≥ 256) and random reads dominate, making the shift cost of sorted insertion unacceptable.
06 HNSW edge selection select-neighbors heuristic (Alg 4): keep e iff d(e,q)<d(e,r) ∀ selected r; keep_pruned backfill Naive nearest-M Heuristic yields diverse, navigable neighborhood (long-range edges); naive nearest-M clusters edges → poor reachability, recall cliff. Naive nearest-M wins when building an index over already-well-clustered data (e.g. synthetic uniform hypercube) where the diversity property is naturally present; it also wins if the build must be 2× faster and the target recall is only ≥ 0.80 (the diversity benefit is most pronounced near recall ≥ 0.95).
07 Vamana pruning robust_prune(p,V,alpha,R), two-pass alpha=1.0 then alpha∈[1.2,1.4] Single-pass; alpha=1 only Occlusion relaxation alpha≥1 adds DiskANN long-range edges; first pass seeds, second relaxes for navigability at bounded R. Single-pass alpha=1 (pure RNG) wins when the data is low-dimensional (≤ 32 dims) or near-uniformly distributed so the strict relative-neighborhood criterion alone produces a navigable graph without long-range edge relaxation.
08 Distance convention Distance(f32), smaller=closer everywhere; IP/Cosine stored negated/1−sim Per-metric comparator; separate max-heaps for IP One ordering rule (asc=nearest) across all heaps/lists/crates → no metric-conditional branches on hot path; kills a whole class of comparator bugs. A per-metric comparator wins in a system that must expose raw similarity scores (not negated) to the caller without the convention-conversion confusion that arises when external APIs expect raw IP/Cosine values — the negation adds a conceptual impedance mismatch at the API boundary.
09 SIMD distance kernels Hand-written AVX2/AVX-512/NEON + runtime feature dispatch via DistanceKernel Compiler autovectorization; scalar-only; external SIMD crate Predictable codegen for L2/IP/Cosine; runtime dispatch ships one binary across ISAs; autovectorization unreliable for horizontal reductions. Scalar kept as differential reference. Compiler autovectorization wins on very high-dimensional vectors (dim ≥ 4096) with modern LLVM: the auto-vectorizer unrolls the reduction loop correctly when the trip count is large and known at compile time, removing the maintenance burden of hand-written intrinsics. External SIMD crate (e.g. packed_simd) wins when targeting multiple exotic ISAs (SVE, RISC-V V) not yet covered by hand-written kernels.
10 Visited tracking VisitedSet = versioned bitset, O(1) per-query reset HashSet<VectorId>; clear-on-reuse bitset Bump generation counter resets in O(1) (no realloc/clear); contiguous bits beat hash probes per node expansion; hashset allocs + cache-misses dominate at high ef. A HashSet wins when the graph order N is very large (≥ 100M nodes) but ef is tiny (≤ 10), so only a sparse fraction of nodes are ever checked — the bitset would be 12+ MB and never fit in L3, while a small hashset stays cache-hot.
11 Determinism Single seeded RNG (BuildParams.seed) for level assignment + tie-breaks Thread-local entropy; non-seeded rand Reproducible graphs required for differential testing vs hnswlib/DiskANN and the optimizer-guarantee gate (compare pre/post recall on fixed sample). Non-seeded / thread-local entropy wins in production when you want entropy-based load spreading across parallel inserts (different seeds per shard reduce hot-node collisions) and reproducibility is provided by the corpus snapshot instead of the build process.
12 Optimizer safety gate Optimizer-guarantee: passes only improve/preserve recall@k at fixed ef on diagnostic sample; degree ≤ R; all nodes reachable from EP post-connect_components Best-effort pruning; trust-the-heuristic Optimization must never silently regress recall or disconnect graph; gate runs RecallHarness+reachability before commit, else rollback. Best-effort / trust-the-heuristic wins when the dataset is so large (≥ 100M) that a recall-harness gate per pass is prohibitively expensive and the heuristic is known-good from offline validation — the gate is replaced by offline A/B recall measurement on a held-out benchmark.
13 Autotuner search CostModel (dist-comps≈ef·mean_out_degree·(1+hop_factor)) + Autotuner: bisection over ef, grid over (alpha,R,L) Bayesian optimization; random search; manual tuning Recall monotone in ef → bisection finds min ef meeting RecallTarget cheaply; coarse grid over build params suffices for Pareto; Bayesian opt adds heavy dep + non-determinism for little gain. Bayesian optimization wins when the tuning space has many coupled parameters (≥ 5) with non-monotone interactions, e.g. a filtered ANN index where ef, R, alpha, filter-selectivity, and re-rank depth all interact — the coarse grid misses the true optimum.
14 On-disk vector compression ProductQuantizer (PQ codebooks + asymmetric distance) Scalar quant (i8); full-precision on disk; OPQ PQ gives DiskANN beam-search-with-rerank memory ratio; asymmetric distance keeps query full-precision; scalar quant lower recall/byte; OPQ deferred. Scalar quantization (i8) wins when vectors have a known bounded range and the precision loss is acceptable (recall@10 target ≤ 0.90) — i8 avoids codebook training, is faster to decode, and fits better into SIMD dot-product instructions (_mm256_dpbusd_epi32).
15 On-disk access mmap_graph (mmap CSR) Read entire graph into RAM; custom page cache Billion-scale graphs exceed RAM; mmap + OS page cache for DiskANN path; full read-into-RAM kept only for small in-memory indexes. Read-into-RAM wins when the graph fits comfortably in RAM and the access pattern is random/unpredictable — mmap's page-fault latency dominates in random-access workloads, while a pre-loaded Vec<u32> has zero fault cost.
16 Delete semantics Tombstones (DeleteSet bitset) + lazy relink, periodic consolidation In-place edge removal; immediate rebuild In-place delete fragments CSR + breaks reachability mid-query; tombstone filters at search, consolidation re-optimizes affected neighborhoods offline. Immediate per-delete rebuild wins in an offline batch scenario where deletes arrive in bulk at a known quiescent period (e.g. nightly) and the recall degradation from stale tombstones over hours is unacceptable — the rebuild cost is paid once, avoiding accumulating recall drift.
17 Recall measurement RecallHarness holds exact brute-force KNN ground truth → recall@k Self-reported overlap; ANN-vs-ANN Exact KNN is the only sound oracle for recall@k; used by diagnostics, autotuner gate, conformance benches. ANN-vs-ANN (comparing two approximate results) wins in production monitoring where computing exact KNN on live queries is too expensive — a shadow-index comparison gives a relative quality signal sufficient for regression alerting when absolute recall is not required.
18 Fuzzing cargo-fuzz (libFuzzer): build/search/optimize never panic Property-test only; no fuzzing Untrusted vectors + degenerate graphs must never panic/UB; fuzz targets per crate boundary. Property-testing only wins when the fuzz corpus is hard to generate meaningfully (e.g. the input space is constrained to semantically valid graphs) and structured property tests with proptest cover the realistic edge cases faster than random fuzzing.
19 Differential oracle hnswlib + DiskANN as reference implementations No external oracle; single self-consistency check Validate recall/topology against canonical impls on identical seeded inputs; catches heuristic drift. No external oracle wins after the implementation is stable and the differential gap is proven small — the hnswlib/DiskANN build dependency becomes a maintenance burden (version pinning, C++ toolchain in CI) that outweighs the detection value once the heuristics are regression-tested in-house.
20 Benchmarking criterion micro-bench + ann-benchmarks recall/latency protocol on SIFT1M/GIST1M/DEEP analogs std::time ad-hoc; custom harness criterion handles variance/outliers for kernel micro-bench; ann-benchmarks methodology for recall@k-vs-QPS curves + regression gates. Ad-hoc std::time wins for a single-run profiling session on a dedicated benchmarking host where variance is low and the measurement overhead of criterion's sampling loop is unwanted — e.g. timing a 30-second full-corpus build where criterion's warm-up budget dominates.
21 CI GitHub Actions: fmt, clippy -D warnings, test, MSRV check, fuzz smoke, bench regression gate Local-only; no MSRV job Enforces production bar + optimizer-guarantee on every push; MSRV job pins 1.78. Local-only CI wins in the early solo-author phase when GitHub Actions minutes are the constraint and the author runs make ci before every commit — the push-CI cost exceeds the benefit until a second contributor joins.
22 MSRV policy Pin 1.78; bump only with ADR + CI MSRV job Track latest stable; no policy Stable feature surface for downstream; explicit bump audit. Track latest stable wins when the project has no external downstream consumers and the team wants access to the newest Rust language features immediately — the stabilization lag of a pinned MSRV blocks const-generic improvements useful in NeighborList.
23 Dependency policy Minimal; NO heavy linear-algebra crate in ann-core (smallvec/bitvec/memmap2 only as needed) ndarray/nalgebra in core; broad dep tree Distance kernels are hand-written SIMD; a BLAS/LA crate adds bloat, opaque codegen, audit surface for zero hot-path benefit. ndarray/nalgebra in core wins when the project also needs matrix operations for PQ codebook training (k-means on sub-matrices), dimension reduction (PCA pre-processing), or learned index training — hand-writing those in SIMD is impractical, and the LA crate pays for itself.
24 Licensing MIT OR Apache-2.0 (dual) GPL; single-license Permissive Rust-ecosystem norm; dual license for max downstream compatibility. GPL wins when the project is a standalone tool (not a library) and the author wants copyleft to prevent proprietary forks — if annopt is only ever a CLI binary, GPL has no downstream embedding cost and ensures derivative work stays open.

10. Repository Layout

Single Cargo workspace, seven ann-* member crates under crates/, one binary (annopt in ann-cli). Directory structure maps 1:1 to the phase list (## 12–## 25): distance kernels → ann-core/distance/, the optimizer passes → ann-optimize/, search engine → ann-search/, persistence/PQ → ann-store/. No code lives at root.

  • vector-ann-graph-optimizer/

    • CONTRIBUTING.mddev loop, MSRV 1.78, fmt/clippy gates, differential-test policy vs hnswlib/DiskANN
    • CODE_OF_CONDUCT.mdcontributor covenant
    • SECURITY.mdreport channel; mmap/untrusted-graph-file threat surface (see docs/threat-model.md)

    • BLUEPRINT_LOG.mdphase completion ledger (Phase 0..13), recall/latency milestones reached

    • Makefilebuild|test|bench|fuzz|conformance|lint|doc targets
