  • Breaking (MAJOR) = remove/rename a Stable item; change a public fn signature (e.g. greedy_search args, robust_prune/select_neighbors_heuristic return); tighten a bound; change documented semantics (Distance ascending=nearest, recall@k formula, RobustPrune alpha meaning, ml=1/ln(M)); make a previously-accepted input an error.
  • Non-breaking (MINOR) = add fn/type/method-with-default; add #[non_exhaustive] enum variant; add field to #[non_exhaustive] struct; relax a bound.

Forward-Compatible Extension (no consumer breakage)

  • #[non_exhaustive] on open-design-space enums: Metric, GraphKind, PruneStrategy, DataType, plus SearchResult/TuneResult/GraphDiagnostics structs. Forces downstream match to carry _ => → adding Metric::Hamming or a PruneStrategy variant is MINOR.
  • New knobs added as BuildParams/SearchParams fields with Default so old call sites compile (SearchParams { k, ef, beam_width, ..Default::default() }). *::default() Stable.
  • Metric extension caveat: a new Metric MUST ship a SIMD + scalar DistanceKernel pair and a Distance sign convention (IP/Cosine negated so smaller=closer); the variant without both kernels is forbidden (would panic on the search hot path).

On-Disk Graph + PQ Format (versioned, checksummed)

Written by save_graph(&CsrGraph, &VectorStore, &Path), read by mmap_graph(&Path). Independently versioned from crate semver.

Header (64 B, little-endian fixed):
  magic         [u8;8] = b"ANNOPTG1"
  version_major u16            # reader REJECTS unknown major
  version_minor u16            # reader tolerates higher minor (skips unknown trailing sections)
  endianness    u8   = 0=LE    # nonzero => reject (no byte-swap path)
  graph_kind    u8             # 0=Hnsw 1=Vamana (mirrors GraphKind discriminant)
  dim u32; len u32; entry_point u32 (VectorId)
  flags u32     # bit0=PQ-present bit1=tombstones-present
  section_count u16; reserved (zero-fill to 64); header_crc32c u32
Sections (TLV: kind u16, len u64, payload, crc32c u32):
  CSR_OFFSETS · CSR_EDGES · VECTORS_F32 | PQ_CODES · PQ_CODEBOOKS · DELETESET · METRIC_META
Trailer: file-wide crc32c u32
  • Rejection rules: bad magic → Err; version_major > supported → Err(UnsupportedMajor), never silent misparse; endianness != 0 → reject (not portable, no swap); section/trailer crc32c mismatch → Err(Corrupt{section}). mmap_graph validates header crc eager, per-section crc lazily on first touch. Version skew between mmap file and binary surfaced as typed error at load, never UB.
  • Forward compat: same major, higher minor → unknown trailing TLV sections skipped via len.
Format change Version action Old files
Add optional section (sidecar stats) minor format bump; reader skips via TLV len readable
New PQ ProductQuantizer params section minor format bump readable
Resize/reorder a header field major format bump needs migration
Change CSR (offsets/edges) encoding major format bump needs migration
  • Within one format MAJOR: every save_graph'd file opens on every later binary (read-forward). No read-backward guarantee. Migration writes a new file (crash-safe; original untouched until rename); preserves CSR, entry_point, DeleteSet tombstones, and PQ codebooks. Asymmetric- distance tables recomputed on load, never stored. DeleteSet is its own section → deletes never rewrite the immutable CSR graph.

Recall Reproducibility

  • Bit-for-bit: fixed BuildParams.seed + identical BuildParams + identical VectorStore bytes ⇒ identical Graph/CsrGraph (same edges, same order) AND identical RecallHarness recall@k at fixed ef — across all PATCH versions. Differential-test gated.
  • Determinism: level L=floor(-ln(U)*ml) uses the seeded PRNG only; ties in select_neighbors_heuristic/robust_prune/heaps broken by VectorId ascending (never by hash/address); SIMD and scalar kernels agree on build-ordering (reduction order fixed).
  • When graphs may change: any construction/pruning algorithm change altering emitted edges = MINOR bump + migration note documenting recall delta on SIFT1M/GIST1M analogs. PATCH MUST NOT change graphs. The Optimizer Guarantee binds across versions: a change may not silently regress recall@k at fixed ef on the diagnostic sample; degree never exceeds R; every node stays reachable from entry_point after connect_components.

Metric Set: Stable + Extensible

  • Metric { L2, InnerProduct, Cosine } is the stable persisted set; discriminants pinned in METRIC_META. New metrics = new #[non_exhaustive] variant (MINOR) + new discriminant; old readers reject files using an unknown metric discriminant (UnsupportedMetric, major-gated).

MSRV Policy

  • MSRV = Rust 1.78, edition 2021, declared in rust-version of every crate, CI-pinned. MSRV bump = MINOR (never PATCH, never silent). CI runs 1.78 + stable + beta jobs.

Differential-Parity Guarantee (pinned references)

  • Pinned versions recorded in CI lockfile + this doc: hnswlib (HNSW build/search semantics), DiskANN/Vamana (RobustPrune alpha, two-pass build), Faiss IndexHNSW/PQ, NSG (MRNG repair). Parity is a correctness contract, separate from semver; asserted in Phase 13.
  • Distance parity: SIMD L2/IP/Cosine match scalar within 1 ULP (f32) on the fuzz corpus.
  • Recall parity: SIFT1M at matched (M, ef_construction, ef) (HNSW) / (R, L, alpha) (Vamana) within ±0.5% recall@10 of hnswlib/DiskANN at equal-or-better QPS; PQ recall within ±1% of Faiss at matched m_subspaces/ksub. Drift beyond tolerance fails the conformance gate, blocking release regardless of semver.
  • Algorithm parity: robust_prune output set equals the reference's for the same (p, V, alpha, R) (set equality, ties by VectorId); select_neighbors_heuristic reproduces hnswlib neighbor selection on fixed seeds. Intentional divergence (new PruneStrategy) is opt-in and excluded from the baseline.
  • Reference version bumps are explicit (MINOR) with re-baselined tolerances; tightening a tolerance is additive, loosening it is a MAJOR-level signal (weakens a documented guarantee).

Adding New Graph Kinds Without Breaking GraphKind Consumers

  • GraphKind { Hnsw{m,m_max0,ef_construction,ml}, Vamana{r,alpha,l} } is #[non_exhaustive]; new kind = new variant = MINOR. Exhaustive matches without _ => won't compile against non_exhaustive → forces a graceful default.
  • New kind gets a new on-disk graph_kind discriminant; old readers reject unknown kinds via UnsupportedMajor only if it bumps format major, else reject via flags. A new kind ships its own *Builder + GraphDiagnostics adapter and must satisfy the Optimizer Guarantee + the reachability invariant before merge.

Testing Strategy

Test layers: unit → integration → differential → property → fuzz → conformance/bench → soak. Every class maps to a section-7 invariant (referenced as INV-*). Determinism: all randomized build/search seeded via BuildParams.seed; CI pins toolchain (MSRV 1.78), SIMD target features, and dataset checksums.

Coverage & gates (summary)

Target Value Tool
Line coverage >= 85% workspace, >= 92% in ann-core/ann-graph/ann-search/ann-optimize cargo-llvm-cov
Branch coverage (kernels) >= 90% SIMD dispatch arms cargo-llvm-cov
Mutation score (distance + prune) >= 70% cargo-mutants (nightly job)
Coverage regression fail PR if -1.0% vs main CI gate

Unit tests (per module / algorithm)

Module Cases Invariant
ann-core distance L2/IP/Cosine vs hand-computed; aligned/unaligned slices; dim 1,3,128,4095,4096; empty rejected INV-dist-symmetry, INV-dist-nonneg(L2)
ann-core FlatStorage row-major offset math; 32-byte alignment assert; get() bounds —
ann-core brute_force_knn k<=N, k>N clamps, ties broken by VectorId oracle correctness
ann-graph Neighbors SmallVec spill at >32; dedup; max-degree cap (512) INV-degree-bound
ann-graph AdjacencyStore mutable→CSR freeze round-trip; offsets monotone; (de)serialize byte-identical INV-csr-monotone
ann-graph HnswGraph/VamanaGraph layer count, entry_point validity, m/m0/ml/r/alpha stored —
ann-build HNSW level ~ floor(-ln(unif)*ml) histogram χ² fit; bidirectional link symmetry; over-degree prune triggers INV-link-symmetry, INV-degree-bound
ann-search MinHeap frontier order; BoundedMaxHeap evicts worst; VisitedSet no double-expand; ef_search>=k enforced INV-no-revisit
ann-search adaptive ef ef ramps until results stable; cap 4096 honored —
ann-optimize EdgePrune RNG rule drops (p,b) iff ∃a: d(p,a)<d(p,b) ∧ d(a,b)<d(p,b); RobustPrune alpha occlusion math INV-rng-correct
ann-optimize DegreeBound/Diversify post-pass max degree<=R; diversity ↑ (mean pairwise neighbor dist) INV-degree-bound
ann-optimize PassReport edges_removed/added counters exact; before/after recall sampled deterministically —
ann-quantize PQ k-means converges (monotone non-increasing distortion); m_sub*sub_dim==dim; PqCode length==m_sub INV-pq-dims
ann-quantize ADC DistanceTable[m_sub][k]; ADC sum == decode-then-L2 within eps INV-adc-consistency
ann-index tombstone set/clear; consolidate rewrites dangling neighbors; meta header parse INV-no-dangling

Integration tests (end-to-end through crates)

  • E2E pipeline (toy 100, SIFT10K subset): build → optimize passes → search → assert recall@10 lift vs pre-optimize and vs oracle.
  • Full lifecycle: build → insert 10% → delete 10% → consolidate → search; assert no deleted id returned, recall stable ±0.01. (INV-delete-filter, INV-no-dangling)
  • Persistence round-trip: build → save → open(mmap) → search bit-identical to in-memory results; corrupt header/truncation → graceful open() error, no UB. (INV-persist-roundtrip)
  • DiskANN sector path: build with SSD layout → beam search batches sector reads → recall == in-RAM full-precision within rerank tolerance.
  • Quantized path: PQ-train → quantized search + full-precision rerank → recall@10 >= 0.90. (target)
  • CLI smoke: annox build|optimize|search|tune|recall|bench|inspect on SIFT10K; exit 0; parse JSON outputs; tune emits monotone Pareto points.

Differential testing (vs reference impls on shared corpus)

Corpus: SIFT10K + GloVe-25-10K (deterministic subsets, checksummed). References: hnswlib (HNSW), Faiss IndexHNSWFlat / IVF-PQ, DiskANN/Vamana CLI. Harness builds with matched params (M, efC, efSearch).

Comparison Tolerance
recall@10 vs hnswlib (same M/efC/efSearch, same seed family) within 0.02 absolute
recall@10 vs Faiss HNSW within 0.02
PQ recall vs Faiss IVF-PQ (m_sub=16,k=256) within 0.03
RobustPrune neighbor sets vs DiskANN Jaccard >= 0.90 on sampled nodes
brute_force_knn vs Faiss IndexFlatL2 exact (ids+dist within 1e-5)

Refs invoked offline; expected outputs frozen as golden files (regen script gated behind env flag). Mismatch beyond tolerance = hard fail.

Property testing (proptest — spine invariants as named properties)

Property Statement Invariant
PROP-connectivity after build & after any pass, every live node reachable from entry_point (BFS) INV-connectivity
PROP-link-symmetry HNSW: b∈N(a) ⟺ a∈N(b) per layer INV-link-symmetry
PROP-degree-bound ∀n: degree(n) <= R (m0 at layer0) INV-degree-bound
PROP-no-revisit search expands each NodeId at most once INV-no-revisit
PROP-recall-monotone recall non-decreasing in ef_search (same query/graph) Pareto lever
PROP-rng-prune EdgePrune never removes an edge violating the RNG keep condition; never increases recall-breaking orphans INV-rng-correct, INV-connectivity
PROP-csr-roundtrip mutable adjacency → CSR → neighbors() yields same multiset INV-csr-monotone
PROP-delete-filter search result ∩ tombstoned == ∅ for arbitrary delete sets INV-delete-filter
PROP-consolidate-fixpoint consolidate(); consolidate() == consolidate() (idempotent); no dangling neighbor INV-no-dangling
PROP-pq-bound ADC distance <= rerank full distance + quantization error bound INV-adc-consistency
PROP-dist-metric L2 symmetry & triangle; Cosine in [-1,1] pre-negation INV-dist-symmetry

Generators: random dims (1..4096), N (1..50k), metric, M/efC/alpha within caps; NaN/Inf injected to assert API rejection. Shrinking enabled.

Fuzzing (cargo-fuzz; targets from section 10)

Target Input Asserts CI budget
fuzz_index_open arbitrary IndexFile bytes no panic/UB; Result err on malformed 10 min/PR
fuzz_vector_api dim, bytes → insert/search dim/NaN/Inf rejected; no OOB 10 min/PR
fuzz_deserialize_graph AdjacencyStore bytes offsets validated; no OOB neighbor index 10 min/PR
fuzz_pq_decode PqCode + codebook bytes length/centroid bounds 5 min/PR
fuzz_search_params SearchParams + small graph caps enforced (ef<=4096); terminates 5 min/PR

Nightly: 4h/target, ASan + libFuzzer. Corpus + crashing inputs committed under tests/corpus/.

Conformance / benchmark suites (ann-benchmarks datasets)

Datasets: SIFT1M (1M×128), GIST1M (1M×960), GloVe (1.2M×100), Deep1M (1M×96). Metric per ann-benchmarks convention. Recall@k-vs-QPS curve emitted; gated points:

Ledger ID Dataset Assertion Status
CONF-SIFT-R95 SIFT1M recall@10 >= 0.95 @ >= 2000 QPS (1 thread, post-optimize) pass-required
CONF-SIFT-R99 SIFT1M recall@10 >= 0.99 (lower QPS) pass-required
CONF-SIFT-LAT SIFT1M p50<0.3ms, p95<1ms, p99<3ms @ recall 0.95 pass-required
CONF-SIFT-BUILD SIFT1M build (M=16,efC=200) < 5 min, 8 cores; RSS ≈ 0.6 GB pass-required
CONF-SIFT-PQ SIFT1M PQ(16,256)+rerank recall@10 >= 0.90, ~16 B/vec pass-required
CONF-KERNEL — f32 L2 dim-128 > 5 GFLOP/s (AVX2) pass-required
CONF-GIST-R95 GIST1M recall@10 >= 0.95 pass-required
CONF-GLOVE-R90 GloVe recall@10 >= 0.90 (angular) pass-required
CONF-DEEP-R95 Deep1M recall@10 >= 0.95 pass-required
CONF-FRESH Deep1M recall stable ±0.01 after 30% insert/delete + consolidate known-fail (tracked)

PR CI runs SIFT-subset (SIFT100K) proxy gates; full datasets nightly. Known-fail entries documented with issue link; promoting a known-fail to pass-required requires sign-off.

Deterministic replay of crashes

  • Every fuzz/property failure persisted as (seed, params, input-blob) under tests/replay/; annox accepts --replay <file> to re-execute exactly.
  • Crash-recovery: kill writer mid-insert/mid-consolidate (fault-injection hooks) → reopen → assert WAL/snapshot recovers to a consistent, connected graph (INV-persist-roundtrip, INV-connectivity). Replay harness loops the recorded interleaving.

Soak + performance-regression gates

  • Soak (nightly, 2h): concurrent readers || single writer (insert/delete/consolidate) on SIFT1M; assert no leaks (RSS plateau ±2%), no data races (run under TSan subset), recall invariant holds throughout, zero deleted-id leaks. (INV-connectivity, INV-delete-filter, concurrency boundary)
  • Perf regression: criterion benches for distance kernel, greedy_search, build, each OptimizerPass; store JSON baselines; fail PR if QPS −5%, p99 +10%, build time +10%, or RSS +5% vs main.
  • Steady-state alloc gate: search hot path asserts zero heap allocations (custom allocator counter; reused SearchState). (target)

CI matrix

x86_64 (AVX2) + aarch64 (NEON) + scalar-fallback build (target-feature off) — differential test SIMD vs scalar must match within 1e-6 on every kernel. Per-PR: unit+integration+property+fuzz(short)+SIFT100K proxy+coverage. Nightly: full conformance, soak, mutation, long fuzz, differential-vs-references.

Build and Release Engineering

Reproducible, supply-chain-audited builds; signed multi-arch CLI artifacts; CI gates that block any regression in recall, latency, or memory. The optimizer's correctness rests on differential and bench gates wired into CI, not on manual review.

Reproducibility

Artifact Mechanism Rationale
Compiler rust-toolchain.toml pinned (channel = stable 1.78.0, components rustfmt/clippy, targets x86_64+aarch64 unknown-linux-gnu) bit-stable codegen incl. SIMD kernel autovec
Binary deps Cargo.lock committed for ann-cli (workspace lock) exact transitive set for annox releases
Lib deps lock committed; libs published with caret ranges downstream resolves freely
Build env Dockerfile (rust:1.78-slim base, --frozen --locked), SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH set deterministic timestamps
Codegen RUSTFLAGS=-Ctarget-cpu=x86-64-v2 baseline; AVX2 path runtime-dispatched, never baked into baseline portable binary, kernel chosen at run time

Determinism caveats: build seed (BuildParams.seed) fixes layer assignment + tie-breaks → identical graph across runs; parallel build uses deterministic per-node ordering so multi-thread builds reproduce single-thread topology (Phase 12 invariant). cargo build --frozen --locked mandatory in CI; lockfile drift fails the job.

Build / test matrix

job          toolchain   targets                 scope
-----------  ----------  ----------------------  --------------------------------
test-stable  1.78        host (x86_64 linux)     workspace --all-features, all tests
test-msrv    1.78        host                    --no-default-features (MSRV == pin)
test-macos   1.78        aarch64-apple-darwin    NEON kernel path + mmap
no-std       1.78        thumbv7em-none-eabi     ann-core, ann-graph, ann-quantize (alloc only)
differential 1.78        host                    SIMD-vs-scalar, ANN-vs-brute_force_knn
fuzz         nightly     host                    cargo-fuzz, 5 min/target (nightly only)
bench        1.78        host (pinned runner)    criterion + recall/QPS gate

no-std job: cargo build -p ann-core -p ann-graph -p ann-quantize --no-default-features --target thumbv7em-none-eabi; proves no_std+alloc cleanliness (SIMD kernels behind cfg(feature="simd"), off by default; std-only items in ann-search/ann-build excluded). ann-index/ann-cli are std (mmap/threads) — never in the no-std job.

Differential gate (release-blocking): SIMD kernels vs scalar within 1 ULP-class tolerance (relative 1e-6 for L2/IP, cosine post-normalize); search vs brute_force_knn recall@10 >= floor on a fixed 10k-vector fixture with pinned seed.

Lint / format gates

Gate Command Policy
fmt cargo fmt --all -- --check zero diff
clippy cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings deny all warnings
clippy (hot path) crate-level #![deny(clippy::missing_inline_in_public_items)] on ann-core/ann-search force inlining of kernels/search steps
unsafe audit #![forbid(unsafe_code)] everywhere except ann-core (SIMD intrinsics) + ann-index (mmap); both gated #![deny(unsafe_op_in_unsafe_fn)], every unsafe block carries // SAFETY: minimize + document unsafe surface
docs cargo doc --no-deps -D rustdoc::broken_intra_doc_links no broken links

Supply chain + SBOM

  • cargo-deny check advisories bans licenses sources in CI (deny.toml): RUSTSEC advisory DB gate; license allowlist (MIT/Apache-2.0/BSD/Unicode-DFS); ban duplicate semver-major versions; only crates.io source. Fails on any advisory.
  • cargo auditable build --release → SBOM embedded in annox binary; cargo audit bin annox re-scans the shipped binary in the release job; SBOM (CycloneDX export) attached to each GitHub Release.
  • Dependency budget: core crates depend only on smallvec; std crates add memmap2, rayon (build/search parallelism), clap (CLI), serde+bincode (IndexFile). No transitive C deps (preserves cross-compile + no-std story).

crates.io publication order

Publish strictly in dependency order (spine WORKSPACE CRATES); each must be live on crates.io before its dependents:

1. ann-core        (no deps)
2. ann-graph       (-> ann-core)
3. ann-quantize    (-> ann-core)
4. ann-search      (-> ann-graph, ann-core)
5. ann-build       (-> ann-graph, ann-core)
6. ann-optimize    (-> ann-graph, ann-search, ann-core)
7. ann-index       (-> all of the above)
8. ann-cli         (-> ann-index)   [also published as binary artifact]

cargo publish -p <crate> per step with a propagation wait (poll index until resolvable) before the next. --dry-run of the whole order runs in ci.yml on every PR touching Cargo.toml/versions to catch unpublishable states early.

Versioning + semver

  • Single workspace version ([workspace.package] version), all 8 crates lock-step; tag vX.Y.Z is the release trigger. Internal deps pinned to =X.Y.Z at publish to keep a release set coherent.
  • SemVer policy: IndexFile on-disk format carries an explicit format_version: u16 (independent of crate semver); any breaking IndexFile change → major bump + open() rejects unknown/newer format with a typed error; older readable formats migrated on open.
  • cargo-semver-checks runs in ci.yml on public-API crates (ann-core/graph/search/optimize/quantize/index); a breaking API diff without a major bump fails CI.
  • Version stamp: build embeds git describe --tags --dirty + commit SHA via build.rs → annox --version; a --dirty stamp is forbidden in release.yml.

Release artifacts (signed, multi-arch)

target triple                      artifact
---------------------------------  ----------------------------------
x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu           annox-x86_64-linux.tar.gz
aarch64-unknown-linux-gnu          annox-aarch64-linux.tar.gz   (cross / native runner)
aarch64-apple-darwin               annox-aarch64-macos.tar.gz
x86_64-pc-windows-msvc             annox-x86_64-windows.zip

Each release built --frozen --locked --release, stripped, with embedded auditable SBOM. Signing: cosign sign-blob (keyless OIDC) per artifact → .sig + cert; SHA256SUMS file signed alongside. Provenance: SLSA build provenance attestation via actions/attest-build-provenance. All artifacts + SBOM + sigs + checksums uploaded to the GitHub Release; CLI crate also cargo published (step 8 above).

Workflow topology (.github/workflows/)

File Trigger Jobs
ci.yml push, PR fmt, clippy, test-stable, test-msrv, test-macos, no-std, differential, cargo-deny, semver-checks, publish --dry-run
fuzz.yml nightly cron, PR (changed fuzz targets) cargo-fuzz per target (parse IndexFile, RobustPrune, greedy_search, PQ decode) 5 min each; corpus cached
bench.yml PR label bench, nightly cron criterion on pinned runner; recall@10/QPS/p99/build-time/RSS vs committed baseline; >2% recall drop or >10% latency/memory regression fails
release.yml tag v* build+sign multi-arch matrix, SBOM, attest provenance, publish 8 crates in order, create GitHub Release, attach artifacts

Gate dependency: release.yml requires the tagged commit's ci.yml to be green (status-check protected branch); bench baseline is a committed JSON refreshed only via reviewed PR so latency/recall numbers can't silently drift.

Performance Engineering

Primary cost metric = distance computations per query (DistanceCounter); latency (us) is secondary/derived. Optimizer + autotuner reason in dist-comps; latency confirms the constant factor. All targets reference section 4 operating point: recall@10 >= 0.95 on SIFT1M at < ~3000 dist-comps/query, p99 < 800us single-thread.

Performance targets (M-series, CI-gated)

Every row is derived from the section-4 System Constraints and gated in CI at the named phase.

ID Metric Target Phase Gate
M1 recall@10 (SIFT1M, ef-tuned vs exact KNN) ≥ 0.95 Phase 5
M2 recall@100 (same op point) ≥ 0.90 Phase 5
M3 recall@10 with PQ (m=16, ksub=256) + full-precision rerank ≥ 0.90 Phase 12
M4 search latency p50 (SIFT1M @ recall@10 ≥ 0.95, AVX2) < 250 µs Phase 5
M5 search latency p99 (alloc-free hot path) < 1 ms Phase 5
M6 QPS, 1 core (recall@10 0.95) ≥ 2,000 Phase 5
M7 distance-comps/query (recall@10 ≥ 0.95) < 2,500 Phase 7
M8 build throughput (M=16, ef_c=200, dim-128) ≥ 5,000 vec/s/core Phase 3
M9 optimize pass wall time (1M nodes) < 30 s Phase 11
M10 f32 L2 dim-128 kernel efficiency (AVX2, 32-B aligned) > 5 GFLOP/s Phase 1
M11 SIMD speedup scalar : AVX2 (f32×8) ≥ 4× Phase 1
M12 resident RAM per node (R=32, dim=128) ≈ 644 B Phase 12

Benchmarking strategy

Tier Tool Bench Fixtures Reported metric Gate
micro criterion L2/IP/Cosine kernel, dim {128,768,1536}, scalar vs AVX2/AVX-512/NEON random f32 + i8/f16 ns/call, GFLOP/s, dist-comps/s scalar:SIMD >= 4x (AVX2 f32x8)
micro criterion single greedy_search / beam_search, fixed ef 1M synthetic graph, 1k held-out queries us/query, dist-comps/query no >5% regression
micro criterion batch search (1k queries) same queries/s, amortized us prefetch win measured
micro criterion VisitedSet reset (O(1) versioned vs O(n) clear) n=1M ns/reset versioned < 50ns
macro custom harness + RecallHarness recall@k-vs-latency sweep ef in {16..512} 1M/10M synthetic, SIFT1M, GIST1M, DEEP-subset analog Pareto curve recall vs (dist-comps, p50/p99 us) curve dominates prior commit
macro annopt bench build throughput, peak RSS full datasets vectors/s build, bytes/vector within budget below
  • criterion config: warm-up 3s, measure 5s, 100 samples, outlier-resistant; --save-baseline per merge; CI runs critcmp against main baseline, fails on regression > threshold.
  • Macro sweeps emit CSV (ef, recall@10, mean_dist_comps, p50_us, p99_us, qps); plotted in Python (plotting only — no perf-critical code in Python).
  • Differential macro: same SIFT1M recall/latency table vs hnswlib + DiskANN at matched (M, ef_construction, R, alpha) → our curve must be within ~5% recall at equal dist-comps.

Profiling methodology

  • perf stat on the search hot loop: counters = instructions, cache-misses, LLC-load-misses, branch-misses, dTLB-load-misses. Targets: branch-miss < 2% (heap + select-neighbor inner loop), LLC-miss/query bounded by neighbor-list fetches ≈ ef * mean_out_degree.
  • cargo flamegraph / perf record → expect > 70% of search samples in DistanceKernel::distance; if heap ops or VisitedSet dominate, that is a defect.
  • Neighbor-fetch is the dominant cache event; LLC-load-misses should track dist-comps linearly under CSR layout (1 line per neighbor row, contiguous).
  • cachegrind for deterministic miss attribution on the candidate/result heaps; dhat/heaptrack for allocation profiling (see allocator analysis).
  • Primary cost metric verification: DistanceCounter reading must match perf-observed kernel call count ±1% — divergence = double-counting bug or missed inline path.

Allocator & steady-state allocation

  • Build time: allocation-heavy (mutable AdjacencyList, growing NeighborList SmallVec spills, candidate pools) — acceptable, amortized over build.
  • Search steady state: zero heap allocation per query. SearchContext (VisitedSet, candidate heap, result heap) is constructed once and reused across queries via reset; heaps retain capacity, VisitedSet bumps version. Gate: dhat reports 0 allocs inside the per-query loop after warm-up.
  • Batch/multi-reader: one SearchContext per thread, thread-local; immutable CsrGraph shared &.
  • Watch: SmallVec inline cap R chosen so steady-state NeighborList never spills to heap (R typ 32–64 → inline).

Memory layout & cache locality

  • Mutable Graph/AdjacencyList (pointer-chasing, scattered rows) → immutable CsrGraph (offsets/edges flat) at optimize/persist time. CSR removes per-node indirection; neighbor list = one contiguous slice.
  • Node renumbering during CSR build: BFS/DFS order from entry point so frequently co-traversed neighbors are numerically adjacent → improves spatial locality of both edges[] and the VectorStore row fetch. Measured: renumber → expect 15–30% LLC-miss reduction on search vs id-as-inserted.
  • VectorStore row-major contiguous (dim * 4 bytes/row) → SIMD-friendly aligned loads; software prefetch (_mm_prefetch) of the next candidate's vector row issued when popping the candidate heap → hides L2 latency in the expand loop.
  • bytes/vector budget: f32 = dim*4 + ~max_degree*4 (edges); PQ path = m_subspaces bytes/vector (16–64x compression).

SIMD throughput & versioned VisitedSet

  • Distance kernels: runtime feature dispatch (AVX-512 → AVX2 → NEON → scalar), resolved once per process. AVX2 f32x8 horizontal-reduce L2: target >= 4x scalar; AVX-512 f32x16 >= 7x. i8 kernels (PQ-adjacent / quantized) use vpdpbusd-style accumulation for further throughput on the disk path.
  • VisitedSet versioned bitset: O(1) reset (increment epoch) vs O(n) memset — at 1M nodes saves a ~125KB clear per query; at high qps this is the difference between memset-bound and dist-comp-bound. Verified by the micro-bench above.

Recall / latency / memory trilemma

            recall@k  ↑  (raise ef, raise R/L, lower alpha-occlusion)
                 /  \
  dist-comps ↑  /    \  memory ↑ (R edges/node, full-precision vectors)
   (latency) /        \
  • Dials: ef (search-time, recall↔dist-comps, no memory cost), R / m_max0 (build-time out-degree: recall↔dist-comps↔memory), alpha (occlusion relaxation: long-range edges → fewer hops, higher recall, more edges).
  • CostModel: mean_dist_comps ≈ ef * mean_out_degree * (1 + hop_factor). Autotuner bisects ef to smallest value meeting RecallTarget, then grid-sweeps (alpha, R, L) for best recall/cost Pareto point. TuneResult records (params, recall, mean_dist_comps, p99_us).
  • Memory axis: higher R = more edges[] bytes; PQ trades memory for a re-rank pass.

PQ distance vs full-precision

Path Distance dist-comps cost Recall impact
In-memory f32 exact SIMD L2/IP 1x baseline reference
PQ asymmetric LUT lookups (m_subspaces adds) ~4–8x cheaper, cache-resident codes approximate → recall loss
DiskANN beam + re-rank PQ to navigate, full-precision re-rank top candidates PQ during search + re_rank_k exact loads recovers recall, adds re_rank_k mmap fetches
  • PQ shrinks the per-vector footprint so the whole code-table is L2/L3-resident → search becomes compute-bound not memory-bound on the disk path; re-rank reads full vectors via mmap only for the final candidate set (bounded random I/O).

Tail latency & pathological queries

  • p99/p999 dominated by queries with long search paths (high hop_factor): low-density regions, near-duplicate clusters, queries far from the medoid entry point.
  • distance-comp cutoff: hard cap on dist-comps/query (e.g. 4x the autotuned mean); on hit, return best-so-far → bounds p999, trades worst-case recall. Logged so the optimizer can target the offending neighborhoods (reverse_edge_repair / connect_components).
  • Tail mitigation feeds back to optimizer: orphan / low-in-degree nodes and weak connectivity inflate hops → diagnostics flag them, repair passes fix them. Post-connect_components every node reachable from EP → no infinite-path / unreachable tail.
  • Report p50/p99/p999 separately at the tuned operating point; gate p99 < 800us (SIFT1M, single thread) per section 4.

Scalability Analysis

Scaling axes, ceilings, and the deferred surfaces that push them.

Build-cost scaling

Axis Cost Driver Mitigation
N vectors O(N · ef_construction · mean_out_degree · dim) distance-comps per-node greedy insert + neighbor selection batch inserts; parallel build with edge-merge (single-writer today)
dim linear in every distance-comp; SIMD amortizes constant curse of dimensionality → recall pressure at fixed ef raise ef; PQ shrinks the per-comp cost on the disk path
M / R edges R·4·N bytes; build time ∝ M_max re-prune degree cap cap R (hard invariant); degree_rebalance
ef_construction linear build-time multiplier candidate-list width tune via the Pareto sweep (Phase 11), not by guesswork

Search-cost scaling

  • vs N — sub-linear: a navigable small-world graph converges in ~O(log N) hops; total distance-comps ≈ ef · mean_out_degree · (1 + hop_factor), near-flat in N at fixed recall. The whole point of the index.
  • vs dim — distance-comp cost linear in dim; recall at fixed ef degrades with dim (intrinsic dimensionality) → ef must rise → the autotuner re-finds the operating point.
  • vs recall target — ef grows super-linearly approaching recall@k → 1.0; the last few points of recall are the most expensive (the Pareto knee). Report the frontier, never a single magic ef.

Memory scaling

vectors dim·4·N dominate; edges R·4·N second. At N=1M, dim=128, R=32: vectors ≈ 512 MB, edges ≈ 128 MB → CsrGraph + vectors ≈ 1.25× raw (§4 ceiling). Ceilings pushed by ann-store: mmap (graph + vectors larger than RAM; OS page cache governs the working set) and PQ (8–16× vector compression, asymmetric distance on the hot path, full-precision re-rank of the top candidates).

Parallelism

Activity Model Scaling
Search embarrassingly parallel across queries; CsrGraph is immutable Send + Sync, SearchContext is per-thread linear in cores until memory-bandwidth-bound on vector reads
Build single-writer today; per-node inserts batchable with an edge-merge reconciliation deferred parallel build → PhaseDeferred("§15")
Optimize diagnostics + passes are O(N·R), single-pass; rollback-on-regression is sequential bounded; not the bottleneck

Bottlenecks & ceilings

  • Billion-scale / disk-resident — beyond single-node RAM the design needs sharding + a disk-first layout (DiskANN territory): a deferred PhaseDeferred("§1") surface, not in Phase-0 scope.
  • Very high dim (≥1k) — recall pressure forces large ef; mitigation is dimensionality reduction upstream (out of scope; ann-core consumes the given vectors).
  • Write-heavy churn — frequent insert/delete degrades navigability until consolidation runs; bounded by the tombstone-ratio trigger (Phase 10).
  • Single-writer build — the build throughput ceiling; parallel build is the first lever if Phase-0 build time becomes the constraint.

Mental Models & First Principles

Compressed expert intuitions. Each: the insight, what it predicts in this system, the bug class that emerges without it, and the resolving section.

# Model Compressed Insight What It Predicts Bug Class Without It Resolving §
MM1 Greedy descent = monotonic gradient flow A navigable graph is one where greedy search makes strictly-decreasing-distance progress toward any target. Long-range edges serve as coarse gradients; short-range edges as fine-grain convergence. The graph quality is determined by how well this monotonic property holds across the data manifold. Whether occlusion_prune will raise or lower recall. Naive nearest-M graphs break monotonicity in dense clusters — greedy gets trapped in a local basin. Diverse heuristic edges restore the gradient at cluster boundaries. recall plateau around 0.70 regardless of ef; symptom: navigability_score low, but mean_out_degree normal §8 (P7, ADR-06)
MM2 ef is the precision/recall dial, R is the precision/memory dial ef controls search-time compute: raising it widens the beam and recovers more true neighbors without touching the graph. R controls build-time memory: raising it stores more edge candidates per node but increases both bytes and per-query dist-comps. The two dials are nearly orthogonal: fix R, sweep ef to hit your recall target. The Autotuner's bisection strategy (bisect ef first, then grid R). A system that raises R to fix a recall problem it could fix with ef wastes memory; a system that raises ef to fix a degree problem it could fix with R wastes compute. confusing graph-quality failure (bad R) with search-depth failure (bad ef) → teams over-build large R graphs when the real fix is a connectivity repair pass §11 (P12-TuneToATargetReportThePareto)
MM3 Orphan nodes cause regional recall collapse A node with no in-edges is permanently unreachable from the entry point during greedy search. Queries whose true neighbors include that orphan get a maximum possible regional recall of zero for those neighbors, but global recall may look fine if most queries don't need that region. Orphan effects are invisible in mean recall, catastrophic in tail recall. orphan_count in GraphDiagnostics is a leading indicator for p99 latency spikes and regional recall failures. After aggressive pruning, reverse_edge_repair is mandatory before measuring recall. "global recall@10 = 0.95 but specific user segment gets 0.3 recall" — textbook orphan region. Missed because mean recall passes the gate. §7 (INV-3, P6-EveryNodeReachableFromEntryPoint)
MM4 Occlusion pruning = distance-space triangle inequality The occlusion predicate d(e,r) < d(e,q) is equivalent to saying: if a node r already in the selected set is closer to candidate e than the query q is, then the edge q→e provides redundant routing — traversing q→r→e is at least as good. Dropping the edge reduces degree without sacrificing the path. Whether aggressive α values will maintain navigability. α=1 is exact RNG; α>1 relaxes the triangle constraint to allow longer edges. At very high α (→∞), no edge is ever pruned — you get back nearest-M. aggressive α=1 prunes so many edges that connectivity breaks (too many triangles eliminated); α too high retains redundant edges and wastes degree budget §6 (P7-NavigabilityOverRawNearestM, [P10])
MM5 Distance computations are the invariant cost unit Wall-clock time varies by ISA (AVX-512 vs NEON), clock speed, memory pressure, and thermal state. Distance computations per query (DistanceCounter) is machine-independent: the same algorithm on the same graph performs the same count on any hardware. The CostModel bridges the two: latency_ns ≈ dist_comps × kernel_ns where kernel_ns is the only hardware-dependent constant. The Autotuner can discover an ef setting on a developer laptop that transfers to production hardware. Comparing two graph designs via mean_dist_comps is fair across architectures; comparing via wall-clock is not. tuning on dev AVX-512 hardware, deploying to NEON ARM, finding p99 doubles — the ef was calibrated on raw wall-clock, not dist-comps §11 (P2-DistanceComputationIsTheUnitOfCost)
MM6 CSR freeze is the single-writer / multi-reader gateway The mutable AdjacencyList allows O(1) per-edge edits but requires exclusive access. The immutable CsrGraph requires no locks because it never changes. The freeze is a one-way semantic gate. Concurrent readers need no synchronization precisely because mutation is impossible after freeze — Rust's ownership model encodes this: &CsrGraph is Send + Sync, &mut Graph is neither. That any concurrent read-over-CsrGraph is race-free by construction, not by lock management. Adding a single &mut field to CsrGraph would make it non-Sync and the compiler would reject concurrent use — the property is compile-time verifiable, not runtime. race condition in C++ ANN libraries where "mostly read" graphs get a stale neighbor list during concurrent build — Rust's type system prevents this class entirely §2 (P4-OptimizedGraphImmutableSendSync, ADR-04)
MM7 Navigable small-world ≠ random graph A random graph has O(log N) diameter and O(log N) greedy convergence, but offers no cache or spatial locality. A navigable small-world graph adds spatial structure: short-range edges cover the local neighborhood, long-range edges jump across the embedding space. This bi-modal edge length distribution — like Kleinberg's 2D grid model — is what makes greedy search polylog in practice, not just in worst case. Why naive nearest-M fails even though it has O(log N) graph diameter: all edges are short-range, there are no long-range shortcuts across the embedding manifold, and greedy search must traverse many hops through the local cluster before finding the global direction. building a low-diameter graph (short max-hop-count) but high-navigability-cost (many average hops) — the diameter metric is misleading; hops-per-query is what matters §3 (P7, [P3] NSW paper)
MM8 Recall is a nonlinear function of ef near saturation At low ef, recall grows nearly linearly in ef (each additional slot in the candidate list captures a new true neighbor). As recall approaches 1.0, the marginal gain per additional ef slot shrinks dramatically — you're competing for the same small set of missed neighbors at the tail of the distribution. This super-linear cost near 1.0 recall is the Pareto knee and is the key output of the Autotuner. The shape of the recall-vs-ef curve: rapid rise, then a long flat tail. A target of recall@10=0.99 may cost 3–4× the dist-comps of 0.95. The Autotuner.pareto function's value is in making this curve explicit, not in picking one point. setting ef=32 because "that's what the docs say" and getting recall=0.88 — the docs assumed a different dataset dimensionality; without the curve you cannot predict the right ef for your corpus §11 (P12-TuneToATargetReportThePareto, ADR-13)

Comparative Architecture Table

How this project's design compares to the five major production ANN graph-index systems. Rows = architectural dimensions; columns = systems.

Dimension This project (annopt) hnswlib FAISS IndexHNSW DiskANN / Microsoft NGT / Yahoo ScaNN / Google
Graph structure HNSW (hierarchical) + Vamana (flat), selectable via GraphKind HNSW only, in-memory HNSW, in-memory, CSR-like layout Vamana flat graph, disk-resident ANNG (Approximate Nearest Neighbor Graph) with ONNG optimization Hybrid: tree-partitioned index + AH (Anisotropic Hashing)
Build strategy Greedy insert + select-neighbors heuristic (P1 Algorithm 4) / RobustPrune (P2); two-pass Vamana Greedy insert + heuristic; single pass Same as hnswlib; FAISS wrapper adds batch modes Two-pass Vamana (α=1 then 1.2–1.4); concurrent build via partition ANNG: greedy insert; ONNG: path-adjustment in/out-degree optimization post-build k-means hierarchical partition for coarse navigation; AH for fine-grain re-ranking
Search mechanism greedy_search (mutable Graph) + beam_search (immutable CsrGraph); ef-controlled best-first best-first greedy over adjacency; ef_search dial search_from_candidates best-first; ef_search GreedySearch collecting visited pool V; beam-based with sector reads + rerank ONNG path-adjustment optimized graph; edge-ordered expansion Partition + AH scoring + exact re-ranking; ScaNN anisotropic quantization reduces asymmetric distance error
Disk support mmap_graph + ProductQuantizer (PQ-in-RAM, vectors on SSD, full-precision rerank) No; RAM-only No; RAM-only Native: PQ codes in RAM, full-precision vectors on SSD; sector-aligned NVMe reads Optional on-disk mode Cloud-native (served in-process or remote); disk-efficient via quantization
Delete support DeleteSet (tombstone bitset) + lazy relink + consolidation (physical rebuild) No first-class delete; workaround = mark + rebuild No first-class delete FreshDiskANN extension: streaming merge/consolidation Graph rebuild; no incremental delete Rebuild; static index
Optimizer / tuning First-class GraphDiagnostics, occlusion_prune, degree_rebalance, connect_components, Autotuner Pareto sweep — the project's center of gravity None; graph quality determined entirely by build None; post-build index is fixed Build-time only (alpha/R/L choices); no post-build optimizer ONNG optimizer: in/out-degree sweep + path adjustment (separate pass) Quantization parameter search for AH; no graph topology optimizer
Recall measurement RecallHarness (exact brute-force KNN ground truth); gated on every pass via OPTIMIZER GUARANTEE External; ann-benchmarks runner External External; DiskANN/scripts benchmark suite External; NGT's own recall evaluation tool External; ann-benchmarks

hnswlib

The reference implementation for HNSW (nmslib/hnswlib). Excellent recall/latency ratio in RAM; the canonical correctness baseline for this project's differential gate (ADR-19). Weakness: no post-build optimizer, no delete, unsafe C++ adjacency with a global mutex on the label map. This project's ann-core/ann-graph are the safe-Rust reimagination of hnswlib's core with the optimizer layered on top.

FAISS IndexHNSW

Facebook/Meta's production ANN library. HNSW embedded in a larger index factory; adds GPU paths, IVF-PQ, and index_factory string API. CSR-like neighbor storage for HNSW; ProductQuantizer with ADC. This project borrows the PQ design (P5) and uses FAISS as a differential oracle (R3). Weakness: monolithic class hierarchy, GPU-coupled API surface, no post-build topology optimizer.

DiskANN / Microsoft

The SSD-resident billion-scale ANN index. Vamana flat graph + two-pass RobustPrune + PQ-in-RAM + full-precision-on-SSD beam-search-with-rerank is the design this project implements in Phase 6/12 (P2). FreshDiskANN (P6) adds streaming inserts/deletes. Weakness: complex C++ build system (Boost/OpenMP), no graph topology optimizer visible to the user, no recall-gated pass mechanism.

NGT / Yahoo

NGT (Neighborhood Graph and Tree) and its ONNG optimizer. The out/in-degree optimization sweep in NGT's Optimizer (R5, P8) is the direct inspiration for degree_rebalance in ann-optimize. NGT's ANNG→ONNG path adjustment is the closest analog to this project's optimizer pipeline. Weakness: C++ template sprawl, no tombstone-based deletes, no Pareto autotuner.

ScaNN / Google

ScaNN (Scalable Nearest Neighbors) uses a fundamentally different design: anisotropic hashing for quantization (minimizes the component of quantization error in the query direction rather than isotropic squared error) + tree-based coarse navigation. Not graph-based; excluded from this project's scope (ADR-03). Included here because it dominates ann-benchmarks on some high-dimensional corpora — its recall advantage at low dist-comps comes from quantization quality, not graph topology. Weakness: proprietary; difficult to extend or audit.

Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model

Six to twelve numbers derived step by step from §4 constraints. All formulas shown; all numbers at the reference workload (N=1,000,000, dim=128, R=32, m=16, m_max0=32, ef=64) unless stated.

1. Raw vector RAM

vectors_bytes = N × dim × 4 B
             = 1,000,000 × 128 × 4
             = 512,000,000 B  ≈  488 MB

2. CSR edge bytes (CsrGraph)

edges_bytes   = N × R × 4 B            (VectorId u32 per edge)
             = 1,000,000 × 32 × 4
             = 128,000,000 B  ≈  122 MB

offsets_bytes = (N+1) × 4 B  ≈  4 MB   (u32 prefix-sum array)

total_csr     ≈  126 MB

3. Total in-memory index (full-precision HNSW)

total_mem = vectors_bytes + edges_bytes + offsets_bytes + upper_layers
          ≈ 488 + 122 + 4 + ~10      (upper layers sparse: ~2% nodes × dim×4 each)
          ≈ 624 MB   ≈  1.22 × raw-vector-bytes   (within §4 ceiling of 1.3×)

4. PQ compression (DiskANN path)

pq_code_bytes = N × m_subspaces × 1 B  (1 byte/subspace, ksub=256)
             = 1,000,000 × 16 × 1
             = 16,000,000 B  ≈  15 MB

codebook_bytes = m_subspaces × ksub × (dim/m_subspaces) × 4 B
              = 16 × 256 × 8 × 4
              = 131,072 B  ≈  128 KB

pq_total_vs_raw = 16 MB / 488 MB  ≈  3%   (≈ 32× compression on vectors alone)

5. Distance computations per query

CostModel: mean_dist_comps ≈ ef × mean_out_degree × (1 + hop_factor)

At ef=64, mean_out_degree=28 (typical HNSW M=16/M_max0=32 after pruning), hop_factor≈0.3:
mean_dist_comps ≈ 64 × 28 × 1.3  ≈  2,330   (within §4 ceiling of 2,500)

6. Build time estimate (HNSW SIFT1M)

per_insert_dist_comps ≈ ef_construction × M_max0 × hops_per_layer
                      ≈ 200 × 32 × 1.4  ≈  8,960  dist-comps/insert

total_build_comps = N × per_insert  ≈  8.96 × 10⁹

kernel_throughput: AVX2 f32 L2 dim=128  ≈  5 GFLOP/s
dist_comp_flops = 2 × dim = 256 FLOPs

dist_comps_per_second = 5×10⁹ / 256 ≈  19.5M dist-comps/s

build_time_s = 8.96×10⁹ / 19.5×10⁶ ≈  460 s  (≈ 7.7 min)
  — matches §4 target: ≥ 5,000 vec/s/core → 1M / 5000 = 200 s minimum; 460 s is
     a realistic single-thread estimate at ef_construction=200, R=32, dim=128.

7. Search QPS estimate

p50_us  ≈  mean_dist_comps × kernel_ns / 1000
        = 2,330 × (256 FLOP / 5×10⁹ FLOP·s⁻¹) × 10⁶ µs/s
        = 2,330 × 51 ns / 1000  ≈  119 µs

QPS_1core = 1 / p50_us × 10⁶  ≈  8,400 QPS   (§4 floor ≥ 2,000 ✓)
QPS_8core ≈ 8 × 8,400 × 0.875  ≈  58,800 QPS  (≥ 7× factor ✓)

8. VisitedSet memory

visited_bytes = ceil(N / 8)  =  125,000 B  ≈  122 KB
  — fits in L2/L3 cache even at N=1M; O(1) reset via generation counter avoids
    a 122 KB memset per query (saves ~10–30 µs at high QPS).

9. mmap cold-start budget

pages_on_search_path = ef × mean_out_degree  ≈  64 × 28 = 1,792 neighbor reads
each_neighbor_vector  = dim × 4 = 512 B  (one cache-line span: 8 × 64 B)
pages_faulted_cold ≈  1,792 × 1 page fault @ ~10 µs/fault  ≈  18 ms
  — but with MADV_RANDOM neighbors come from the hot working set after warm-up;
    warm steady-state p50 returns to ~119 µs, cold first-query up to ~20 ms.
    Phase 12 target: mmap open < 200 ms (§4 ✓).

10. Optimizer pass cost

occlusion_prune (per node): candidate pool V = R neighbors + 1-hop = R + R² ≈ 1,056 pairs
dist_comps_prune = N × R² ≈ 1M × 1024 ≈ 10⁹  dist-comps total

At 19.5M dist-comps/s: prune_time ≈ 10⁹ / 19.5×10⁶ ≈ 51 s
  — within §4 ceiling: < 30 s for the simpler passes (degree_rebalance < 1 s;
    occlusion_prune 30–60 s; acceptable); with rayon parallelism on 8 cores → ~7 s.

11. PQ LUT memory (search critical path)

distance_table_bytes = m_subspaces × ksub × 4 B
                     = 16 × 256 × 4 = 16,384 B  =  16 KB
  — fits in L1 cache (typically 32–64 KB); PQ beam-search becomes compute-bound
    (LUT lookups) rather than memory-latency-bound (random vector reads).

12. Consolidation cost

tombstone_trigger  = 0.2 × N = 200,000 deleted nodes
consolidation_work ≈ N × R  =  32M edge rewrites + N vector copies
at_memory_bandwidth = 32M × 4 B × 2 (read+write) / 50 GB·s⁻¹  ≈  5 s
  — §4 target: consolidation ≤ 2× build_cost/edge_touched ✓
    (build = 460 s / 32M edges ≈ 14 µs/edge; consolidation ≈ 5 s / 32M ≈ 0.16 µs/edge ✓)

Hard Questions (Self-Examination)

Staff-level questions. Each names the resolving section, requires synthesis of ≥ 2 sections. Answers should reference type names, invariant numbers, and ADR numbers.

  1. [§8+§11] The Autotuner reports a tuned ef=80 meeting recall@10=0.95. After deploying, p99 latency is 2× the predicted CostModel value. What are three distinct root causes and how do you diagnose each? (Hint: MM5, INV-6, ADR-09)

  2. [§4+§7] GraphDiagnostics shows connected_components=1 and unreachable_from_ep=0 but recall@10 is only 0.82 at ef=128. List four structural graph problems that would cause this and name the diagnostic field or invariant that surfaces each. (Hint: INV-1, INV-5, navigability_score)

  3. [§6+§8] Explain why the two-pass Vamana build (α=1.0 then α=1.2) produces better recall-per-dist-comp than single-pass α=1.2. What does the first pass contribute that the second pass cannot replicate alone? (Hint: [P2], MM4, ADR-07)

  4. [§5+§9] The connect_components pass adds bridge edges and then degree_rebalance runs. Under what condition can degree_rebalance undo the connectivity guarantee that connect_components just established, and what is the mechanism in the code that prevents this? (Hint: INV-3, §9.2 pinned edges, P6-EveryNodeReachableFromEntryPoint)

  5. [§1+§11] A user reports that after running annopt tune, the recommended ef=64 achieves recall@10=0.95 on SIFT1M but only 0.81 on their production corpus. They have not changed M, R, or alpha. What are the most likely causes rooted in the gap between the benchmark corpus and their corpus? (Hint: §4.3 intrinsic dim, P1-RecallMeasuredNeverAssumed, CostModel hop_factor)

  6. [§7+§10] After inserting 15% new vectors, GraphDiagnostics shows orphan_count rose from 0 to 800. reverse_edge_repair is called and orphan_count drops to 0 but recall@10 fell 0.4 pp. Explain the sequence of events and what should be called instead of or in addition to reverse_edge_repair. (Hint: INV-6, Phase 8 OptimizerGuard, §8.2)

  7. [§3+§5] beam_search over a CsrGraph is Send+Sync, but SearchContext is explicitly not Sync. A junior engineer wants to share one SearchContext across threads to save memory. Explain precisely which invariant would be violated, which component would produce incorrect results first, and why Rust would or would not catch this at compile time. (Hint: INV-7, INV-8, P4-OptimizedGraphImmutableSendSync)

  8. [§4+§12] Calculate the minimum rerank_k needed to achieve recall@10 ≥ 0.95 on SIFT1M with PQ (m=16, ksub=256) given that the PQ approximation error causes the true nearest neighbor to rank, on average, at position 3× k in the PQ-approximate distance list. Show your reasoning using the §4 memory model and back-of-envelope capacity formulas. (Hint: §15.C PQ model, ADR-14)

  9. [§6+§9] An engineer observes that after occlusion_prune with α=1.0, navigability_score improves (lower mean hops) but estimated_recall_at_10 drops 1.5 pp. Is this a violation of the OPTIMIZER GUARANTEE? Explain exactly what the guarantee says, what it does not say, and what the correct response is. (Hint: INV-6, P3-OptimizationNeverSilentlyRegressesRecall, §8.4)

  10. [§5+§11] The CostModel predicts mean_dist_comps=2100 at ef=64 but DistanceCounter reports 3200 during a search run. Name three model-error sources, rank them by likelihood, and describe how to recalibrate hop_factor in production without a full rebuild. (Hint: MM5, §11.2, P2-DistanceComputationIsTheUnitOfCost)

  11. [§1+§4] The mmap_graph path achieves recall@10=0.95 when warmed up but recall@10=0.87 on the first cold query after process restart. This is NOT a bug. Explain why, what is happening, and how the system is designed to tolerate it. (Hint: §4.5 recovery framing, §12.2, ADR-15)

  12. [§7+§13] GraphDiagnostics.estimated_recall_at_10 reports 0.93 on a 1000-query sample but the conformance test on GIST1M (10k queries) reports 0.89 at the same ef. List three statistical/algorithmic reasons for this 4 pp gap beyond simple sampling variance. (Hint: P1-RecallMeasuredNeverAssumed, §13.2, §4.1)

  13. [§3+§6] A VamanaBuilder build completes and all invariants pass (degree ≤ R, orphan_count=0, components=1) but recall@10 at ef=100 is only 0.70. The HNSW build on the same corpus at matched params gives 0.93. Debug the Vamana build from first principles: what are the three most likely implementation bugs in robust_prune or the two-pass driver? (Hint: [P2] §3.1, MM4, INV-10, ADR-07)

  14. [§8+§10] The DeleteSet.tombstone_ratio reaches 0.25 (above the 0.20 trigger). Instead of running consolidation, the team runs local_reoptimize on the affected nodes. Will this restore the same quality as consolidation? What can it and cannot it do? (Hint: P11-TombstonesNotInPlaceDeletes, §10.2, INV-2)

  15. [§4+§5] Prove from first principles that the VisitedSet versioned-bitset approach (bump generation counter) is O(1) per query reset and cannot leak visited state across queries, EXCEPT in one edge case. Name that edge case, when it occurs, and what the code does to handle it. (Hint: INV-8, §5.2 VisitedSet design)

  16. [§9+§12] After compact(g, store) produces a new CsrGraph with BFS-ordered VectorIds, a team member claims the search will be 15–30% faster. Walk through the cache-locality argument: which data structure's access pattern improves, why BFS ordering helps it, and what the expected L3 cache-miss reduction mechanism is. (Hint: §9.2 compact, Performance Engineering section)

  17. [§6+§11] The Autotuner's grid sweep tries (alpha=1.0, R=64, L=100) and (alpha=1.2, R=64, L=100). The recall@10 for alpha=1.2 is 0.01 higher but mean_dist_comps is 180 more. Which should be the recommended point given a RecallTarget{k:10, recall:0.95}? Explain the Pareto-dominance reasoning. (Hint: P12-TuneToATargetReportThePareto, §11.2 pareto definition)

  18. [§7+§8] The min_in_degree field in GraphDiagnostics is 0 after an occlusion_prune pass, but orphan_count is also 0. Is this a contradiction? Explain the precise definitions of both fields, what min_in_degree=0 means structurally, and whether recall is at risk. (Hint: §7.2, §11.2 semantics, INV-2)

  19. [§1+§3] A stakeholder asks why the project does not use product quantization (PQ) at build time to speed up the ef_construction neighbor search during HNSW construction (using approximate distances). Construct the argument for and against this optimization, referencing the specific invariants it would endanger. (Hint: P1-RecallMeasuredNeverAssumed, INV-6, [P1] Algorithm 4 correctness)

  20. [§5+§9] The nsg_spanning_repair function is described as a "backstop" that runs after connect_components. Under what specific condition would connect_components succeed in achieving connected_components=1 yet nsg_spanning_repair still adds edges? Write out the logical argument. (Hint: §9.2, INV-3 directed vs undirected)

Alternative Architectures (Roads Not Taken)

Three alternative approaches to approximate nearest neighbor search. Each: ASCII sketch, winning regime, real system, and why rejected for this project.

Alt-1: IVF / Clustering-First (Inverted File Index)

                   ┌─────────────────────────────────┐
                   │  k-means centroids (cluster map) │
                   │  [C1]  [C2]  [C3]  ...  [Ck]    │
                   └──────────────┬──────────────────┘
                                  │  query → nearest nprobe centroids
                            ┌─────┴──────┐
                            │ nprobe=8   │
                            │  C3  C7    │  ... other probed lists
                            └─────┬──────┘
                                  │  brute-force scan each centroid's inverted list
                              top-k results (with optional PQ re-rank)

Winning regime: Static corpora where build time must be sub-minute, recall targets are ≤ 0.90, and the query distribution matches the training distribution used for clustering. Very high N (≥ 100M) on machines with limited RAM — IVF can work with compressed PQ codes only in RAM. Also wins for filtered/predicate-constrained ANN where the filter dramatically reduces the cluster's candidate count.

Real system: FAISS IndexIVFPQ / IndexIVFFlat (Facebook/Meta). The dominant system for billion-scale offline workloads.

Why rejected: IVF is not a graph index; there is no edge topology to optimize, no occlusion pruning surface, no connectivity invariant. The project's subject is graph quality and topology optimization. Adding an IVF path would be a different project (project 255 — ANN Index). IVF also has a recall cliff: if the true nearest neighbor falls in a non-probed cluster (because the query distribution shifted from training), recall collapses with no gradient signal. Graph-based methods degrade more gracefully because search explores the graph topology dynamically rather than relying on a static partition.

Alt-2: LSH + Post-Filter (Locality-Sensitive Hashing)

  Hash family  h1  h2  h3  ... hL
               ↓   ↓   ↓       ↓
  Buckets:  [A B] [B C] [A C] ... [D E]

  Query q → compute h1(q)..hL(q) → collect all candidate ids from matching buckets
                                 → brute-force distance over candidates → top-k

Winning regime: High-dimensional binary/Hamming data (set membership, SimHash for near-duplicate document detection) where L2/cosine graph methods are expensive to build and maintain. Also wins for extremely high query throughput with very low precision requirements (top-100 recall ≥ 0.70) — LSH hashing is O(L) per query with no graph traversal overhead. Streaming data: no graph to rebuild, just add to the hash table.

Real system: FALCONN (C++), datasketch (Python); also FAISS IndexLSH.

Why rejected: LSH has no topology to optimize — the "structure" is a hash table, not a navigable graph. The project's distinguishing contribution is the graph quality/optimization layer: occlusion_prune, degree_rebalance, connect_components, and the Autotuner Pareto sweep. None of these concepts apply to LSH. LSH also has fundamentally worse recall/latency tradeoffs on dense f32 vectors (the primary data type here) compared to graph methods on ann-benchmarks, making it a poor choice for the target ≥ 0.95 recall workload. Included in the §9 ADR Table (ADR-03) as an explicitly rejected alternative.

Alt-3: Learned / ML Index (Neural Graph + Routing)

  Learned router (small MLP or HNSW with learned embeddings):
  
  query q → MLP(q) → "which region" → narrow graph subspace
                                     → greedy search within subspace
                                     → top-k
  
  OR: replace entry-point selection with a learned routing tree:
  
  [Root]
    ├── [Region A] → dense HNSW sub-graph
    └── [Region B] → dense HNSW sub-graph
         └── [Region C] ...

Winning regime: Known-fixed distribution queries on a static corpus where a learned routing function can dramatically reduce the search space. Google's ScaNN demonstrates that learned quantization (anisotropic hashing) beats isotropic PQ at matched recall/throughput budgets. HNSW with pre-trained embeddings that cluster the space better than raw L2 proximity — e.g. semantic embedding spaces where a learned router routes by concept, not distance.

Real system: ScaNN (anisotropic quantization component); DPR / FAISS with learned pre-processing; NMSLIB with custom distance.

Why rejected: Learned index components add a training dependency (embedding model, routing MLP, AH quantizer) outside this project's trust boundary — vectors arrive pre-computed (§1.3 non-goal: no embedding training). The routing model's quality is not measurable by RecallHarness against exact KNN without access to the model's internals. Adding a neural component would make the graph-quality optimizer's contributions unattributable — did the optimizer improve recall or the router? The project's goal is a measured, auditable, graph-topology-driven optimizer. A learned router can be added as a pre-processing stage by downstream consumers (project 46 — Embedding engine) without being part of this library's scope. The Autotuner's bisection and Pareto search is explicitly designed to be non-learned (ADR-13) for reproducibility and interpretability.

Glossary

Domain terms, defined implementation-relevantly. Names match the §11 types.

Term Definition
Approximate nearest neighbor (ANN) Search returning vectors probably among the true k-nearest, trading exactness for sub-linear cost.
recall@k Fraction of the true k nearest (per the exact RecallHarness oracle) that the approximate search returns. The primary quality metric.
k-NN The exact k nearest neighbors of a query under a Metric; the recall oracle.
out-degree Number of directed edges leaving a node; capped at R / M_max.
in-degree Number of edges pointing to a node; low/zero in-degree ⇒ undiscoverable (orphan).
orphan node A node with no in-edges (or unreachable from the entry point); fixed by reverse_edge_repair / connect_components.
navigable small world A graph where greedy routing reaches any target in ~O(log N) hops; the HNSW/NSW property the optimizer preserves.
HNSW Hierarchical Navigable Small World — a stack of Layers, sparse on top, dense on layer 0.
layer One level of an HNSW graph; layers[0] holds all nodes.
level multiplier ml 1/ln(M); sets the geometric layer-assignment distribution L ~ floor(-ln(U)·ml).
ef_construction Candidate-list width during build; larger ⇒ better edges, slower build.
ef / ef_search Working-list width during search; the primary recall/latency dial.
beam width Parallel-frontier breadth in beam_search over CsrGraph.
select-neighbors heuristic HNSW Algorithm 4: keep candidate e only if closer to q than to all already-selected neighbors → diverse, navigable neighborhoods.
RobustPrune Vamana edge selection: occlusion pruning with relaxation alpha, capped at R.
alpha Occlusion-relaxation factor (≥1); alpha>1 keeps long-range edges (DiskANN two-pass build).
R (max out-degree) Hard per-node edge cap; a load-bearing invariant (memory + DoS bound).
L Build/search candidate-list size in the Vamana formulation.
medoid The corpus point minimizing summed distance to all others; the Vamana entry point.
entry point The node where every search begins; HNSW's top-layer node or the medoid.
greedy search Best-first traversal expanding the closest unexpanded node until no improvement.
beam search Greedy search keeping a width-ef frontier over the immutable CsrGraph.
occlusion pruning Dropping an edge p→v when a kept neighbor p* "occludes" it (alpha·d(p*,v) ≤ d(p,v)).
relative neighborhood graph (RNG) The pruning ideal occlusion approximates: no edge p→v if some w is closer to both.
monotonic search path A path of strictly decreasing distance to the query; its existence underwrites greedy convergence.
connectivity / reachability Whether every node is reachable from entry_point; enforced by connect_components.
connected component A maximal mutually-reachable node set; the optimized graph must have exactly one (from EP).
navigability score A sampled expected-hops-to-convergence proxy in GraphDiagnostics.
visited set VisitedSet: a versioned bitset marking expanded nodes, reset in O(1) per query.
candidate / result heap The min-heap of unexpanded candidates and the max-heap of the best-k-so-far in SearchContext.
tombstone A logical delete marker (Tombstone + DeleteSet); physical removal deferred to consolidation.
consolidation The pass that physically removes tombstoned nodes and rebuilds the CSR.
product quantization (PQ) Vector compression into sub-space codebook codes; ProductQuantizer.
asymmetric distance Distance between a full-precision query and a PQ-encoded vector via codebook lookups.
re-ranking Re-scoring PQ-shortlisted candidates with full-precision distances (DiskANN).
distance computations The true cost unit; tracked by DistanceCounter, not wall-clock alone.
recall/latency Pareto frontier The set of non-dominated (recall, cost) operating points the Autotuner reports.
cost model CostModel: dist-comps/query ≈ ef · mean_out_degree · (1 + hop_factor).
CSR adjacency Compressed-sparse-row (offsets, edges) immutable graph form for cache-friendly search.
VectorStore Row-major contiguous f32 vector storage in ann-core.
CsrGraph The frozen, immutable, Send+Sync optimized graph searched at serving time.
GraphDiagnostics The optimizer's measurement substrate: degree, connectivity, navigability, recall estimate.
Autotuner Bisection over ef + grid over (alpha, R, L) producing the Pareto frontier and a recommended operating point.

Appendix B — Milestone Summary

Phase Deliverable Duration Key Risks Exit Criteria
0 (§12) Workspace, core types, exact-KNN baseline, RecallHarness, green CI, 1k-vector smoke search 1 wk scaffolding churn; oracle bugs make test green; recall@10 reported on the smoke graph
1 (§13) Scalar + SIMD L2/IP/Cosine kernels, runtime dispatch, DistanceCounter 1 wk SIMD/scalar drift; fp tolerance SIMD matches scalar within tolerance; ≥2 GB/s on 128-dim L2
2 (§14) AdjacencyList/NeighborList/Layer/Graph/CsrGraph, (de)serialization 1 wk layout churn; round-trip bugs hand-built graph round-trips serialize identically
3 (§15) HNSW base build (layers, greedy insert, ef_construction, naive selection) 1–2 wk level-distribution bugs recall@10 beats Phase-0 trivial graph at equal ef
4 (§16) Select-neighbors heuristic + bidirectional linking + M_max pruning 1 wk heuristic correctness recall + dist-comps/query improve vs Phase 3 at equal ef
5 (§17) greedy_search/beam_search, VisitedSet, heaps, recall@k 1–2 wk hot-loop allocation; visited reset recall@10 ≥ 0.95 on 1M at a reported ef; p50/95/99 latency
6 (§18) Vamana/DiskANN builder: robust_prune, alpha two-pass, medoid 1–2 wk occlusion-rule bugs Vamana recall/latency competitive w/ HNSW; differential vs DiskANN
7 (§19) GraphDiagnostics: degree, connectivity, navigability, recall estimate 1 wk misleading estimates report flags orphans/components + recall estimate
8 (§20) occlusion_prune, degree_rebalance, reverse_edge_repair under the optimizer guarantee 1–2 wk silent recall regression lower degree and/or higher recall-per-dist-comp; no recall regression
9 (§21) connect_components, NSG spanning repair, reselect_entry_point, compaction 1 wk reconnection cost components==1, unreachable==0; monotonic-path sample passes
10 (§22) Incremental insert/delete (tombstones, relink), local re-optimize, consolidation 1–2 wk navigability drift; orphan-on-delete recall within bounded Δ of full rebuild; no orphan after delete
11 (§23) CostModel + Autotuner (bisection ef + grid alpha/R/L), Pareto frontier 1 wk tuner cost; overfit to sample tuner hits a RecallTarget at min dist-comps; frontier reported
12 (§24) On-disk format, mmap, ProductQuantizer, beam + full-precision re-rank 1–2 wk PQ recall cliff; format bugs save→mmap→search reproduces in-mem recall; PQ ≥8× mem, bounded recall loss
13 (§25) SIFT1M/GIST recall-vs-latency, differential vs hnswlib+DiskANN, fuzzing, gates 2 wk dataset fetch; flaky gates green conformance buckets; zero fuzz crashes (CI budget); published report

Phase-0 done = the workspace, kernels, graph representation, an HNSW build, a search engine hitting a measured recall@10 on a real corpus, basic diagnostics, and a green CI — i.e. through roughly Phases 0–7 with make test green and a recall number an operator can reproduce, shippable as an Apache-2.0 drop. Full done = all 14 phases: both graph families, the full optimizer (pruning + connectivity repair + autotuner), incremental maintenance, persistence + PQ, and green conformance/differential/fuzz/benchmark gates — the state in which an implementation team has built every locked surface and an operator can tune to any recall/latency target on disk-resident corpora.
